                                                          Re: Trained Observers
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Mon, 31 Aug 2009 08:40:21 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 01 Sep 2009 06:37:24 -0400
  Subject: Re: Trained Observers

  >From: Ray Dickenson <r.dickenson.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Mon, 31 Aug 2009 01:00:46 +0100
  >Subject: Re: Trained Observers
  >>From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>Date: Wed, 26 Aug 2009 14:28:51 -0400
  >>Subject: Re: Trained Observers
  ><snip>
  >>Is this the general consensus on this List? Does having multiple
  >>sightings make one a less reliable observer? Isn't this a bit
  >>like shooting the messenger?
  >Yes, but looking at 'trace evidence' cases, and studies of
  >close-encounter witnesses seems to show there's at least two
  >catch-22s involved.
  >First, many UFOs are surrounded by, or emanate, strong e.m
  >fields, including hard radiation. So the sense-organs of a
  >close-encounter witness could be influenced. And if the witness
  >has had multiple sightings, further effects might be anticipated
  >or suspected.
  Do you know of witnesses who have had multiple close encounters?
  My understanding is that most of the multiple _sequential_
  sightings involve high-flying lights in the sky - unlikely to
  cause biological effects that would handicap the observer. These
  two kinds of sightings may have entirely different explanations.
  >Second, studies of witnesses seem to show character change
  >following a close encounter. Witnesses will say "It's changed my
  >life/the way I think" - another 'catch-22' for an investigator.
  That sounds similar to some near-death experiences which are
  also reported to be life-changing. This kind of reaction would
  be expected if the driving force behind the sighting were the
  witness's own consciousness. If the unknown object were created
  by the opening of a safety valve in an observer's own psyche,
  one might expect a profound shift in that person's outlook on
  life. It would be natural for such active involvement in the
  sighting to be followed by a strong emotional, even numinous,
  response.
  If a witness has experienced more than one sighting, it's
  possible that s/he is more than just a passive observer. This
  could be an important aspect of this class of sightings.

  William

  From: Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>

  Subject: MUFON Presentation

  I would like to thank MUFON for giving me the 2009 EBE Award
  For Excellence In UFO Investigation.
  I presented my article entitled From McMinnville To
  Stephenville: 35 Years Of UFO Imagery Analysis, which can be
  found in the Proceedings of the 2009 MUFON Symposium.
  I have now added to my web site my power point presentation at
  the Symposium.
  Go to:
  http://brumac.8k.com/
  and scroll down a short distance to the paragraph that mentions
  the presentation and read or download the .ppt file with the
  above name.
  Enjoy!

    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 1

                                    P-47 - Dick Hall And The Journal Of UFO History
  From: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  Date: Tue, 01 Sep 2009 10:20:00 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 01 Sep 2009 10:20:00 -0400
  Subject: P-47 - Dick Hall And The Journal Of UFO History



  From: Jan Aldrich <project1947.nul>
  To: PROJECT-1947.nul
  Date: Fri, 28 Aug 2009 09:28:04 -0400
  Subject: Dick Hall And The Journal Of UFO History

  There will be one more issue of the Journal of UFO History. It
  will be a tribute to Dick Hall and will finish off subscriptions
  owed to all JUFOH subscribers. A small number will be available
  for sale.
  Discussions are underway about The Journal of UFO History after
  the "final" issue. It is hoped that it will continue and become
  an on-line journal with contributions from various UFO
  historians
  The final print version will be about 60 pages with a tribute to
  Dick Hall and about 50 pages of UFO history from Airships to the
  UFOs in the New Millennium, two themes of Dick's many writings.
   Contributions of little known historical UFO documents,
  incidents, and updates of UFO incidents in UFO history are
  welcome as are interesting letters, incidents involving various
  personalities in the UFO field, especially Donald E. Keyhoe,
  and, of course, little known items from the National
  Investigations Committee on Aerial Phenomena (NICAP) which was
  Dick Hall's passion. In addition to the two volumes of "The UFO
  Evidence" and the successful "Uninvited Guests" which was
  published in several languages Dick wrote many monograms and
  reports such as the two, "Airships to Arnold" and "UFO Sighting
  in the New Millennium" which are sort of book ends that
  encompassed his articles, books and reports on the UFO
  phenomenon and its history.
  In your musty files are probably newspaper clippings, reports,
  historic government documents which few have seen. Donald Todd
  who became interested in UFO in 1947 sent me an account of his
  UFO sighting in December of 1947. The only other person,
  outside his family and friends, he shared this with was Leonard
  Springfield. This is the kind of material compiled together
  which will be a fitting tribute to Dick.
  Please send any submission to
  Jan Aldrich
  P. O. Box 391
  Canterbury, CT 06331
  or
  project1947.nul

                                                        Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 18:54:21 +0100
  Archived: Tue, 01 Sep 2009 14:10:38 -0400
  Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  >From: Jim Deardorf <deardorj.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 08:20:37 -0700 (PDT)
  >Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 11:58:04 +0100
  >>Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>>Bright UFO Passes Commercial Jet: Captured From Inside The
  >>>Aircraft?
  >>>Examiner.com
  >>>http://tinyurl.com/mauzrw
  >>This is a very nice film of a subsun, a reflection of the sun
  >>in
  >>a layer of plate-like ice crystals below the aircraft.
  >>The effect appears on the same azimuth as the sun. If all the
  >>crystals were perfectly horizontal the reflection would be
  >>perfectly circular; but usually they drift at various small
  >>angles from the horizontal which elongates the image
  >>vertically.
  >>Martin Shough
  >The problem there, Martin, is that the intensity of the
  >"subsun"
  >should have fluctuated radically as it alternately passed along
  >the domes and gaps of the alto-cumulus-like cloud tops below.
  >But it didn't.
  There is no problem, Jim. The clouds you identify as alto-
  cumulus are middle clouds generally in the range 6000 - 20,000
  ft. They generally won't be ice clouds and also in general
  because these are convective clouds in vertically unstable air
  any ice crystals in them are not going to be orientated in
  regimented strata so as to produce a subsun.
  Subsuns are produced in thin layers of cirrus that form usually
  above 26,000 and are always ice. The plane is probably at a
  cruise altitude of around 30,000ft. If the clouds of ice
  platelets are in stable air they will tend to lie horizontally
  below the aircraft and mirror the sun as described.
  If you search specialised sites like atoptics.com you will find
  images of subsuns looking exactly like this formed in exactly
  these conditions. The haze of cirrus crystals can be thin and
  itself virtually imperceptible.
  >The lack of contextual information to go along with so many of
  >these videos these days is appalling. If they had supplied the
  >flight information, one could at least estimate if the sun was
  >where you supposed it to be.
  I agree that the poverty of information is (typically)
  appalling. I'd be much happier to be supplied with chapter and
  verse. As it is, viewing the video and looking at the shadows on


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m01-012.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:19]
Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  the cumulus tops indicates a direction of illumination from
  somewhere in front of the camera rather than behind. The
  presence of a phenomenon in the approximate dirtection of the
  sun and looking and behaving in every respect like a subsun
  seems to be reasonable evidence that the sun is indeed where it
  appears to be. This leads me to predict that if and when any
  more detailed information is forthcoming it will prove the
  subsun theory at nearer the p = 1.0 level. That's testable. What
  is your prediction?

  Martin Shough

  the Nova Scotia coast.
  Incidentally, I was under the impression that the lighthouse in
  relation to Rendlesham was regarded as one of the dumbest

  Hopeless.

                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 2

                                                        Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  From: Jim Deardorff <deardorj.nul>
  Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 14:55:30 -0700 (PDT)
  Archived: Wed, 02 Sep 2009 10:15:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  >From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 18:54:21 +0100
  >Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>From: Jim Deardorff <deardorj.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 08:20:37 -0700 (PDT)
  >>Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>>From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  >>>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>>Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 11:58:04 +0100
  >>>Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>>>Bright UFO Passes Commercial Jet: Captured From Inside The
  >>>>Aircraft?
  >>>>Examiner.com
  >>>>http://tinyurl.com/mauzrw

  >>This is a very nice film of a subsun, a reflection of the sun
  >>in a layer of plate-like ice crystals below the aircraft.
  >>>The effect appears on the same azimuth as the sun. If all the
  >>>crystals were perfectly horizontal the reflection would be
  >>>perfectly circular; but usually they drift at various small
  >>>angles from the horizontal which elongates the image
  >>>vertically.
  >>>Martin Shough
  >>The problem there, Martin, is that the intensity of the "subsun"
  >>should have fluctuated radically as it alternately passed along
  >>the domes and gaps of the alto-cumulus-like cloud tops below.
  >>But it didn't.
  >There is no problem, Jim. The clouds you identify as alto-
  >cumulus are middle clouds generally in the range 6000 - 20,000
  >ft. They generally won't be ice clouds and also in general
  >because these are convective clouds in vertically unstable air
  >any ice crystals in them are not going to be orientated in
  >regimented strata so as to produce a subsun.
  >Subsuns are produced in thin layers of cirrus that form usually
  >above 26,000 and are always ice. The plane is probably at a
  >cruise altitude of around 30,000ft. If the clouds of ice
  >platelets are in stable air they will tend to lie horizontally
  >below the aircraft and mirror the sun as described.
  Martin,
  Actually the clouds resemble lower-level alto-cumulus more than
  cirro-cumulus. But in case you're right, around 30,000 ft, you'd
  still need to dig up some example of where the subsun's
  intensity was very constant whether there was solid white cloud
  directly underneath/behind it, or just invisible ice crystals
  showing blue sky, in a case when the airplane traversed a
  combination of both. I've never heard of a steady subsun


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m02-006.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:23]
Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  existing under such a condition myself. I believe that this is a
  detail worth paying attention to rather than ignoring. (The
  atoptics.com website you referred to seems irrelevant.)
  When you see a cloud layer like that, of an alto-cumulus or
  cirro-cumulus type, you know that (weak) updrafts formed and
  maintain the domes along with (weak) downdrafts between. Those
  downdraft areas are not conducive to the survival of any thin
  ice-crystal layer; they're clear and cloud-free because the
  cloud substance evaporated as the sinking air warmed. What you
  need is a cirro-stratus layer of nearly constant opacity. You
  can look up a 1998 paper in JAS on subsuns by J. I. Katz, for
  example: "From an airplane it is sometimes possible to see a
  nearly specular reflection of the sun from a flat layer of
  stratus clouds..." For a long-lasting subsun image, you need a
  stratiform cloud.
  Granted other characteristics of that unidentified image looked
  like a subsun, but if a primary characteristic didn't, then
  the subsun explanation is out. We've both been exposed to lots
  of UFO sighting reports where the sighting resembled some
  known object or phenomenon, but wasn't, if the witnesses'
  details were correct; e.g., resembling an airplane, but making
  no sound and/or wrong navigational lights, or no tail, etc. So
  they're not IFOs. In the present case, one can't say the
  witness was wrong to find this out of the ordinary and worthy
  of sending to some UFO news service; the detail I've pointed
  out is there for all to see. Neverthess, if no witness comes
  forward, we can forget it.

  Jim

  >crystals were perfectly horizontal the reflection would be
  >perfectly circular; but usually they drift at various small
  >angles from the horizontal which elongates the image vertically.

  Martin,
  Could you elaborate a little on your verdict?
  It would be instructive to have your word on:
  1. Why this explanation delivers an image that has a 'pacing'
  effect:
  i.e. appearing to move along with the aircraft, retaining its
  positional orientation vis-a-vis internal visual reference
  points.
  2. In light of this pacing effect, why you rule out a reflection
  from an internal source.
  3. The rationale for your assertion that the effect appears on
  the same azimuth as the sun: I'm assuming that you get to this
  from an analysis of shadows on clouds and the reflection on the
  inner rim of the front of the engine casing; I'd have said this
  data supports 'similar' azimuth, rather than 'same'. What I
  don't know is whether that distinction makes much difference to
  the verdict.
  4. The likely reason for the fade-out of the image.
  5. Whether the ice crystals in question are forming at an
  altitude that would be similar to that for those that deliver
  lunar halo phenomena sometimes visible from the ground
  (connected, in my mind at least, with the infrequency query,
  below)
  My reason for asking you to these questions is straightforward.
  We have an image here that fits, superficially at least, a
  substantial number of verbal sighting reports from aircraft. I
  think quite a few list members would find it valuable to know
  more, if only to better calibrate their evaluation of and
  attitude to such reports.
  Further to that point, I wonder just how common this phenomenon

Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  is. In particular, whether its infrequency would be sufficient
  to mislead experienced aviators into attributing it to an
  entirely different type of cause.

  >>>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>

  >>>Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>>>From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  >>>>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>>>Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 11:58:04 +0100
  >>>>Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>>>>Bright UFO Passes Commercial Jet: Captured From Inside The
  >>>>>Aircraft?
  >>>>>Examiner.com
  >>>>>http://tinyurl.com/mauzrw
  >>>This is a very nice film of a subsun, a reflection of the sun
  >>>in a layer of plate-like ice crystals below the aircraft.
  >>>>The effect appears on the same azimuth as the sun. If all the
  >>>>crystals were perfectly horizontal the reflection would be
  >>>>perfectly circular; but usually they drift at various small
  >>>>angles from the horizontal which elongates the image
  >>>>vertically.
  >>>>Martin Shough
  >>>The problem there, Martin, is that the intensity of the "subsun"
  >>>should have fluctuated radically as it alternately passed along
  >>>the domes and gaps of the alto-cumulus-like cloud tops below.

  >>There is no problem, Jim. The clouds you identify as alto-
  >>cumulus are middle clouds generally in the range 6000 - 20,000
  >>ft. They generally won't be ice clouds and also in general
  >>because these are convective clouds in vertically unstable air
  >>any ice crystals in them are not going to be orientated in
  >>regimented strata so as to produce a subsun.
  >>Subsuns are produced in thin layers of cirrus that form usually
  >>above 26,000 and are always ice. The plane is probably at a

  >Martin,

  >cirro-cumulus.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m02-011.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:25]
Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  Jim, please read my previous email again more carefully. Yes,
  the fluffy cloud tops visible below the plane are indeed
  probably altocumulus, so-called middle clouds, as you say at a
  much lower level than cirrus ice clouds. That is exactly what I
  said. I explicitly pointed out that these were *not* cirriform
  clouds.
  >But in case you're right
  I am right about that. So are you.
  >around 30,000 ft, you'd
  >still need to dig up some example of where the subsun's
  >intensity was very constant whether there was solid white cloud
  >directly underneath/behind it, or just invisible ice crystals
  >showing blue sky, in a case when the airplane traversed a
  >combination of both.
  To the extent that I can understand this, you appear to be
  saying that an intervening ice crystal layer at say 27,000 ft (a
  few k/ft below the aircraft) could not exist above a cumuliform
  cloud layer at say 10,000 ft (20,000 ft below the aircraft). I
  don't know on what grounds you would wish to assert such a
  thing, or why you think that if there were an ice crystal layer
  below the plane it would be seen against "blue sky".
  I've never heard of a steady subsun
  >existing under such a condition myself. I believe that this is
  >a detail worth paying attention to rather than ignoring.
  What conditions? What am I ignoring?
  >(The atoptics.com website you referred to seems irrelevant.)
  Atoptics.com is not at all irrelevant. It is a very useful site
  maintained by an acknowledged expert in atmospheric otpics (Les
  Cowley) which you would do well to study in order to understand
  what a subsun is. Or there a hundred others.
  >When you see a cloud layer like that, of an alto-cumulus or
  >cirro-cumulus type, you know that (weak) updrafts formed and
  >maintain the domes along with (weak) downdrafts between. Those
  >downdraft areas are not conducive to the survival of any thin
  >ice-crystal layer; they're clear and cloud-free because the
  >cloud substance evaporated as the sinking air warmed.
  Again, this is precisely what I told you, to correct _your_
  previous assertion that the phenomenon could not be a subsun
  because the reflection would have fluctuated as the line of
  sight (LOS) tracked across those lumpy cumuliform cloudtops.
  Those clouds are irrelevant. The reflection is occurring from
  platelet ice crystals many thousand of feet above those clouds.
  >What you need is a cirro-stratus layer of nearly constant
  >opacity.
  No, incorrect. What you need is hexagonal plate-like ice
  crystals. The thickness of the layer or the "opacity" is
  irrelevant. The reflection geometry is the same for any ice
  crystal in the LOS no matter how far away from the eye it is and
  the image can be formed from crystals falling in a deep region
  of air at many disfferent distances from the eye (the layer or
  cloud is _optically_ thin, but can be metrically thick). As long
  as the number of crystals in the line of sight is roughly
  constant and as long as they continue to drift with the right
  average (horizontal) orientation then the reflected sun image
  will stay constant. When the LOS passes into a region of fewer
  crystals or of convectively disordered crystals then the image
  will fade away.
  >You can look up a 1998 paper in JAS on subsuns by J. I. Katz,
  >for example: "From an airplane it is sometimes possible to see a
  >nearly specular reflection of the sun from a flat layer of
  >stratus clouds..." For a long-lasting subsun image, you need a
  >stratiform cloud.
  You actually don't need perceptible cloud at all. You can often
  see right through a halo-forming region of ice crystals as though
  it wasn't there. This is more obvious when it happens at low
  altitude with clear landscape in the background. See e.g.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m02-011.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:25]
Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  http://www.atoptics.co.uk/fz120.htm.
  When the right type of ice crystals form at low level in extreme
  cold they're called "diamond dust", but they are the same as the
  cirrus crystals that usually form around -40C at high altitude
  (see for example
  http://cloudappreciationsociety.org/march-09/)
  >Granted other characteristics of that unidentified image looked
  >like a subsun, but if a primary characteristic didn't
  All characteristics do
  >then the subsun explanation is out.
  so it's in.
  >We've both been exposed to lots
  >of UFO sighting reports where the sighting resembled some
  >known object or phenomenon, but wasn't, if the witnesses'
  >details were correct; e.g., resembling an airplane, but making
  >no sound and/or wrong navigational lights, or no tail, etc. So
  >they're not IFOs.
  I agree, but you need to have a very strong evidence indeed that
  the red light really was on the wrong wing before concluding
  that a description resembling an aircraft in every other way was
  not of a real aircraft. If there is any analogous counter-
  evidence in this case I don't know what it is. As far as I can
  see, so far, it looks like a subsun and quacks like a subsun.
  >In the present case, one can't say the
  >witness was wrong to find this out of the ordinary and worthy
  >of sending to some UFO news service
  Who suggested that "the witness was wrong"? It's a good video of
  a very interesting phenomenon which, as you say, is out of the
  ordinary and worthy of sharing. But it would seem perverse of
  you to be offended on the witness's behalf because, having
  shared it, s/he is offered an explanation. Is this not why
  people report things? To try and understand what they see?
  >the detail I've pointed out is there for all to see.
  What detail would that be?
  >Neverthess, if no witness comes forward, we can forget it.
  Well, it is still a very interesting record of an unusual
  phenomenon that few of us get to see directly and worth
  remembering on that account
  Martin

    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 3

                                                        Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  Date: Wed, 2 Sep 2009 17:56:01 +0100
  Archived: Thu, 03 Sep 2009 06:21:42 -0400
  Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  >From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
  >Date: Wed, 2 Sep 2009 10:40:52 +0100
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>From: Martin Shough <parcellular.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>Date: Tue, 1 Sep 2009 11:58:04 +0100
  >>Subject: Re: News Links - 30-08-09
  >>>Bright UFO Passes Commercial Jet: Captured From Inside The
  >>>Aircraft?
  >>>Examiner.com
  >>>http://tinyurl.com/mauzrw
  >>This is a very nice film of a subsun, a reflection of the sun
  >>in
  >>a layer of plate-like ice crystals below the aircraft.
  >>The effect appears on the same azimuth as the sun. If all the
  >>crystals were perfectly horizontal the reflection would be
  >>perfectly circular; but usually they drift at various small
  >>angles from the horizontal which elongates the image
  >>vertically.
  >Martin,
  >Could you elaborate a little on your verdict?
  OK, Gerald.
  >It would be instructive to have your word on:
  FWIW!
  >1. Why this explanation delivers an image that has a 'pacing'
  >effect:
  >i.e. appearing to move along with the aircraft, retaining its
  >positional orientation vis-a-vis internal visual reference
  >points.
  For the same reason that a rainbow "follows" your car. The image
  is a transaction between the sun and your eye that is mediated
  by a changing set of raindrops / ice crystals. The fixed points
  are eye and sun; the reflectors come and go.
  >2. In light of this pacing effect, why you rule out a
  >reflection
  >from an internal source.
  In light of this pacing effect, why would I rule out a subsun?
  It looks just like a subsun. The conditions appear to be
  consistent with a subsun.
  A subsun appears to the eye to be "out there" and objective, and
  would readily attract someone to film it as a UFO. It stays "out
  there" in the same apparent position even if you move your head
  around. But as you know, an image in a mirror appears to be the
  same distance behind the mirror as the source is in front of it.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m03-001.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:27]
Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  An interior window reflection would be revealed as such by
  moving your head because of obvious parallax.
  Then there is the question of what the source could be. Thinks
  about the angle the LOS makes with the surface of the window. As
  can be seen from the window frame, engine pods and lower cloud
  deck the LOS is looking somewhat forward and down with the
  camera not far fromn the glass. Knowing the basic geometry of
  incidence and reflection, and knowing airline seating, that
  tells you that the light source would have to be somewhere on
  the back of the seat immediately in front of the photographer.
  What could it be? And why wouldn't it be obvious to the
  photographer that such an unusual light in this position was the
  source of a reflection in the window?
  Maybe you can argue that the photographer knew it was a window
  reflection but deliberately wanted to make a film of a "UFO".
  But really it's just much simpler to conclude that a light which
  has the shape, appearance and behaviour of a subsun, is a
  subsun.
  >3. The rationale for your assertion that the effect appears on
  >the same azimuth as the sun: I'm assuming that you get to this
  >from an analysis of shadows on clouds and the reflection on the
  >inner rim of the front of the engine casing; I'd have said this
  >data supports 'similar' azimuth, rather than 'same'. What I
  >don't know is whether that distinction makes much difference to
  >the verdict.
  Maybe I wasn't clear but "the effect appears on the same azimuth
  as the sun" refers to the effect called a subsun. Whether the
  image in question is exactly on the azimuth of the sun or not
  can't be determined, but judging from the cloud shadows the
  azimuth of sun and object seem likely to be "similar" (as you
  say) and could be identical. That is, the cloud shadows are not
  inconsistent with the subsun theory
  >4. The likely reason for the fade-out of the image.
  Either the plane moves beyond the region of ice crystals (most
  likely IMO) or the stable horizontal orientation of the crystals
  breaks down for some reason (less likely IMO because the subsun
  would then elongate further in a vertical direction just before
  disappearing - I don't detect that) or else the sunlight
  incident on the ice crystals is blocked by higher cloud (least
  likely, considering the altitude and visible conditions).
  >5. Whether the ice crystals in question are forming at an
  >altitude that would be similar to that for those that deliver
  >lunar halo phenomena sometimes visible from the ground
  >(connected, in my mind at least, with the infrequency query,
  >below)
  The crystals responsible can form at any altitude where it's
  cold enough. Up at cirrus altitudes it's around -40C, but they
  can form very much lower at around -20C, so even down to ground
  level in some conditions (ice fogs of diamond dusts). Different
  shaped crystals can also form that cause different halos. There
  is no physical differemnce between moon halos and sun halos,
  just a different light source.
  >My reason for asking you to these questions is straightforward.
  I hope my answers are, too.
  >We have an image here that fits, superficially at least, a
  >substantial number of verbal sighting reports from aircraft. I
  >think quite a few list members would find it valuable to know
  >more, if only to better calibrate their evaluation of and
  >attitude to such reports.
  >Further to that point, I wonder just how common this phenomenon
  >is. In particular, whether its infrequency would be sufficient
  >to mislead experienced aviators into attributing it to an
  >entirely different type of cause.
  I don't know how to estimate the frequency of potential subsun
  sightings, but I shouldn't have thought they were rare given how
  often there will be clear sunny sky above planes routinely
  flying at ~30,000ft in conditions for cirrus crystals to form.
  I'm sure there have been cases where aircrews have reported


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m03-001.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:27]
Re: News Links - 30-08-09

  subsuns as unknowns, but I can't myself bring one to mind right
  now. Case 54 from the Condon Report was "inconclusively"
  identified as a subsun photographed from a plane at >20,000ft
  http://www.ncas.org/condon/text/case54.htm
  Martin Shough

                                                        Re: Roswell Regurgitated
  From: Gildas Bourdais <bourdais.gildas.nul>
  Date: Thu, 3 Sep 2009 11:29:57 +0200
  Archived: Thu, 03 Sep 2009 07:33:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: Roswell Regurgitated

  >From: Steven Kaeser <steve.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>,
  >Date: Wed, 2 Sep 2009 11:59:12 -0400
  >Subject: Re: Roswell Regurgitated
  >>From: Gildas Bourdais <bourdais.gildas.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>Date: Wed, 2 Sep 2009 11:43:01 +0200
  >>Subject: Re: Roswell Regurgitated
  ><snip>
  >>The only miracle I see here is that people like you keep
  >>claiming that the Roswell officers were lured by balsa wood
  >>sticks and metallic sheets.
  ><snip>
  >I find it somewhat surprising that people believe that Marcel
  >would have been fooled by a standard RAWIN balloon, or even
  >classified Mogul balloon fragments - which I don't believe were
  >of exotic unknown materials.
  >I would be interested to know if anyone can find a quote from
  >Charles Moore linking his Mogul project to the Roswell incident
  >prior to his being visited by James McAndrews of the in the
  >mid-90's while he was compiling The Roswell Report: Case Closed
  >for the Air Force.
  >Charles Moore seemed to change his position after that meeting,
  >as I recall, and later wrote a Roswell book to explain how the
  >Mogul project was the cause.

  There have been some variations in the official statements on
  Mogul.
  One fact that everyone can check is that, in the big Roswell
  Report of 1995, the NYU complete report on the Mogul flights
  states that Mogul 5, launched on June 5,1947, was the first
  complete balloon train launched at White Sands.
  Mogul 4 is not mentioned at all in that report, and Charles
  Moore was one of the authors!
  In that same report, Mogul 2 and 3 are listed as failed attempts
  in Bethlehem, Penn, but the Air Force presentation mentions a
  launching of Mogul 3 at White Sands on May 29.
  In his book of 1997, Charles Moore states clearly, on page 82,
  that the balloon ascension of May 29 did not have the recording
  instruments, which had not yet arrived, so it was not a Mogul
  flight.
  The bottom line is that there was no Mogul 4 flight at all, and
  the story written by Moore in his book is an invention.

  Gildas Bourdais

  Dear List:
  Couple of points:
  Charles B. Moore claimed he never knew the name of his project.
  In the first AF report the project Moore was working on was
  always referred to as the New York University Balloon Project.
  Therefore, I wonder whether the name Mogul had anything to do
  with a project now linked with Charles B. Moore and Roswell.
  The material I found suggests that Mogul was a project that took
  place in Alaska. The only documentation I could find was a memo
  involving Maurice Ewing with a memo subject line stating "Mogul
  and Torrid."
  Ewing's research was involved with developing technology for


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m04-001.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:30]
Re: Roswell Regurgitated

  monitoring the upper atmosphere sound channel. Balloons were
  incidental to the project and under the control of someone else.

  KK

  times telling us that they did know the name of the project back
  then. And there is a letter written a few years later in which


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m04-004.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:31]
Re: Roswell Regurgitated

  Moore is introduced to Dr. James A. van Allen in which the name

  Project it is clear that Moore forgot it.

  >always referred to as the New York University Balloon Project.
  >Therefore, I wonder whether the name Mogul had anything to do

  took place in New Mexico and New Jersey... or at least the
  various documents tell us that some of it was done in those
  places.
  Just a couple of thoughts on this... and a question as to why
  this was even mentioned, given all the information that is
  available on Mogul.

  See, this is the kind of strategy that just doesn't work
  anymore:
  http://tinyurl.com/ndmxs5

  sightings and higher close encounters like there's no tomorrow
  and of course the Invalidation Nation shows up with every cheesy
  attempt to undermine and shy people away from reporting and
  investigating these events.
  This one however is really scraping the bottom of the barrel
  because it's the type of, pardon the pun, filler that news sites
  love because they revel in such foolishness.
  However, I think this one story, pardon the pun, is going to
  backfire on them just as much as the rash of Chinese Lantern
  excuses.
  So basically I'm saying if Rendelsham was due to manure I would
  like to be the first to say this latest story and it's confessor
  Peter Turtill are full of more shit than a constipated elephant.

  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Fri, 04 Sep 2009 19:21:01 +0100
  Archived: Sat, 05 Sep 2009 09:45:05 -0400
  Subject: Re: Rendelsham UFO Sighting Was Manure Truck On

  I've just come off the phone from Peter Turtill, he was very
  friendly and helpful.
  I asked him two main questions; how was he sure about the date,
  and what kind of fertiliser was in the truck.
  He told me that he couldn't be sure about the date, and it was
  chemical fertiliser in the truck, not bullshit!
  He also mentioned that the truck remained outside Sutton Heath
  airfield for about 20 years before it was finally removed.
  Sutton Heath appears to be an old name for Woodbridge RAF base.
  I think we can safely say that though his story is probably
  true, it isn't related to the actual events in question.

                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 5

                                 Re: Rendelsham UFO Sighting Was Manure Truck On
  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Sat, 05 Sep 2009 13:27:26 +0100
  Archived: Sat, 05 Sep 2009 10:55:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: Rendelsham UFO Sighting Was Manure Truck On

  Chemical fertiliser usually contains high volumes of Nitrogen. By
  igniting the fertiliser, Mr. Turthill created a huge cloud of
  Nitrous Oxide which passed through the forest just as the party
  entered it on the first night to investigate the flames from the
  fire.
  According to Wikipedia:
  http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitrous_oxide
  'Nitrous oxide (N2O) is a dissociative drug that can cause
  analgesia, depersonalization, derealization, dizziness, euphoria,
  and some sound distortion.
  Since the earliest uses of nitrous oxide for medical or dental
  purposes, it has also been used recreationally as an inhalant,
  because it causes euphoria and slight hallucinations'.
  Case solved - now on to Roswell!

  Joe

Re: Suggestions On The Roswell Time Line




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 5

                                           Re: Suggestions On The Roswell Time Line
  From: Gildas Bourdais <bourdais.gildas.nul>
  Date: Sat, 5 Sep 2009 18:04:03 +0200
  Archived: Sat, 05 Sep 2009 13:06:48 -0400
  Subject: Re: Suggestions On The Roswell Time Line

  To EBK and the List,
  The following suggestions regarding the time line of the Roswell
  event are based essentially upon the outstanding book of Tom
  Carey and Donald Schmitt Witness to Roswell. That book is a must
  read for anyone interested, especially the second edition with
  still new witnesses, published this year. However, I am not
  completely satisfied with their time line, and I have made some
  suggestions to modify it a bit, already in 2008. Here is, for
  your appraisal, an abridged version of my article visible, in
  French, on my blog at:
  http://bourdais.blogspot.com/2009/06/roswell-nouveaux-temoins-2009
  Before Monday July 7, 1947 : rumors of a crash
  An important point, which appears so well in the book of Carey and
  Schmitt, is that, before the turning point of Tuesday, July 8, with
  the famous press release, a lot of people in the area and in the
  city of Roswell were already talking of the crash of a "flying
  saucer".
  Among the neighbors of Brazel (see page 75 of the new edition):
  Proctor, Sultemeier, Strickland, Martin, Eppers, Pierce,
  Boswell, and McKnight. And also, people from the Richards ranch,
  just south of the Foster ranch (pages 50, 51).
  Many had visited the debris field and picked some debris. Some
  had seen the bodies near the debris field (page 47): Timothy
  "Dee" Proctor, Sidney "Jack" Wright, two sons of rancher Thomas
  Edinton, and one daughter of Truman Pierce.
  Some people were showing around pieces of debris, already at the
  rodeo in Capitan, on Friday 4.
  Brazel had informed some people in Corona on Saturday : his
  uncle Hollis Wilson, customers at the Wade's bar, the general
  store, his friend, the police officer Robert Scroggins (who may
  have shown later a piece of memory metal to the firemen of
  Roswell). And his landowners, the Foster brothers in Texas,
  according to Geraldine Perkins : he did it from her store
  because she had he only telephone line!
  There are also witnesses at the city of Roswell, and at the
  base, according to whom the rumor was running of a saucer crash.
  Therefore, it is quite probable that the officers of Roswell and
  their hierarchy became aware of that situation, and that it led
  them to issue the press release as a damage control operation,
  on Tuesday morning.
  Let's see more closely how things may have happened on Monday 7
  and Tuesday 8. I follow their book, but suggest a few changes.

  Monday July 7, 1947
  The two sites on the Foster ranch : debris field and body site
  On Monday 7, Major Marcel and Captain Cavitt inspect the debris


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m05-008.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:35]
Re: Suggestions On The Roswell Time Line

  field.
  Since Brazel had already spoken to Franck Joyce on Sunday of the
  nearby body site (let's call it the third site), it seems very
  probable that he also led them to that site, although he son of
  Marcel does not believe it because his father never talked to
  him about it. But Marcel revealed later to other witnesses that
  he had seen alien bodies (Sgt Herschel Grice, and Sue Marcel
  Methane). The bodies at the third site were badly injured, and
  smelling.
  Could the Roswell base send troops to take control of the third
  site already on Monday evening?
  We know that Cavitt returned to the base earlier than Marcel, who
  remained on the debris field. There are reasons to suppose that it
  would have been decided then to go back urgently to the third site,
  to take back the bodies, even before taking control of the area of
  the Foster ranch. Brazel was not available then to lead them,
  having been brought back to Roswell by Walt Whitmore, but
  Cavitt could do it.
  The second site, or impact site, closer to Roswell
  The archeological team found the second site, with craft and
  bodies, probably on Monday morning.
  According to Carey and Schmitt, they called the authorities in
  Roswell after walking some five miles to find a phone in the
  hamlet of Mesa, on the road 285. That took some time, evidently.
  We don't know at what time the police and military reached the
  site. It is plausible that it took them also a bit of time to
  find the place. So, it seems probable that this operation of
  taking control happened only in the afternoon, or early evening.
  It seems that they decided not to bring back the bodies
  immediately, having soon gained control of the site, smaller
  than the debris field and closer to Roswell. They decided to
  install them in a tent for the night. Two witnesses, Ed Sain and
  Raymond Van Why, told their families that they had been posted
  to guard the tent for the night. Both said that they had seen
  the craft, so it had to be at the second site, closer to
  Roswell. This seems confirmed by the duration of travel to the
  site, less than an hour.
  This decision not to bring back the bodies immediately may have
  been taken because of sanitary risk (already perceived as
  critical with the deteriorated bodies of the third site). The
  testimony of the photographer Benthal, brought the next morning,
  seems to confirm that (obliged to wear a protective suit,
  noticing a smell of formaldehyde in the tent, seeing a
  refrigerator truck.). On the other hand, the survivor would have
  been brought back to the base as soon as Monday evening, in an
  ambulance.
  Tuesday, July 8
  Briefing of Colonel Blanchard, at 7.30 am.
  If General Ramey and Colonel DuBose were there, as revealed by
  Walter Haut, they probably had arrived on Monday evening, and
  they returned to Fort Worth as soon as Tuesday morning after
  the briefing, to prepare the press meeting with a weather
  balloon in the afternoon.
  Marcel and Cavitt make their report. At this time, all must be
  worried by the risk of not being able to control rapidly enough
  the site at the Foster ranch, hence the decision to issue a
  rather vague press release in the morning, in order to be able
  to look good if they failed. At the briefing, that decision is
  made, evidently, in liaison with the Pentagon.
  Toward the end of Tuesday morning, the military are able to
  cordon the whole area. The risk of leak in the public is
  considerably reduced, and it becomes possible to organize the
  balloon story at Fort Worth in the afternoon.
  Bringing the craft and bodies to the base
  The craft is brought back to the base during Tuesday, as told by
  several witnesses


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m05-008.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:35]
Re: Suggestions On The Roswell Time Line


  The bodies of the second site, after the photographic session,
  are brought to the base during Tuesday, at the end of the
  morning, or early afternoon.
  Eli Benjamin, a new important witness, was requested in the
  afternoon to go at the big hangar P3. Once there, he had to
  carry bodies to the hospital, where he saw medical and non
  medical officers already waiting for them. These were probably
  the bodies just brought from the second site.
  At the hospital, Benjamin noticed a foul smell, a detail which
  seems to confirm that the smelly bodies of the third site were
  already there, for an autopsy. Carey and Schmitt also give the
  credible testimony of Miriam Andrea Bush who confirmed a medical
  examination. Her testimony, together with some others, seems to
  confirm the controversial testimony of Glenn Dennis
  If this time line is correct, Montoya would have seen the
  bodies, in the hangar P3, sometime on Tuesday. Was it in the
  morning or in the afternoon? One question is: how could he enter
  the base, which was supposedly locked at that time? Perhaps
  because there was a lot of agitation during the whole day, and
  some lack of command, many officers being already out, at the
  sites, or on their way to Fort Worth.
  According to still other witnesses, in the city of Roswell and
  on the base, the craft seems to have been brought in the
  afternoon, at the hangar P3. Montoya did not mention it, but
  Walter Haut said that Blanchard went with him to see it in the
  hangar, and also the bodies, presumably later in the afternoon.
  These testimonies seem compatible.
  Transportation of the craft, the debris, the bodies and the
  survivor.
  It seems that the smelly bodies, after an aborted autopsy at the
  hospital, were secured in a distant area, under a tent for the
  night of Tuesday to Wednesday, before being loaded in a crate in
  the B-29 "Straight Flush", on Wednesday.
  There is some uncertainty remaining for the transportation of
  the bodies of the second site. According to the testimony of
   General Blanchard, they were sent first to Muroc (now Edwards
  AFB), and later to Wright field, in a plane piloted by Captain
  Henderson.
  There is uncertainty as to the transportation of the living
  being, and of the remains of the craft.
  According to Lt.col Magruder, they were carried to Wright field,
  since he told his sons that he had seen them there around mid-
  July (actually later according to the second edition). The
  transportation of the craft would have been made by road,
  presumably.
  In short, the new testimonies seem to blend well together with
  the older ones, to make a credible story, and a strong case for
  the reality of a UFO crash near Roswell.

  Gildas Bourdais

  However, the only material I found that mentions Project Mogul
  is UFO researchers quoting other UFO researchers.
  Whereas I attempted to trace a piece of information back to its
  source.
  Yes, the Air Force decided in its second report to address
  ufologists' insistence on the term Mogul.
  I decided the AF research team was following orders and did not
  mention any information having to do with the real Mogul
  project.
  So, I agree with everybody there is a cover-up but we disagree
  just what information is being covered up.

US Dept. of State Dead Bodies Cabinet




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 6

                                              US Dept. of State Dead Bodies Cabinet
  From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  Date: Sun, 6 Sep 2009 19:21:26 +0000
  Archived: Sun, 06 Sep 2009 18:09:22 -0400
  Subject: US Dept. of State Dead Bodies Cabinet

  From the book Ghost, Confessions Of A Counterterrorism Agent, by
  Fred Burton:
  Chapter: Human Poker Chips, page 67.
  The dead bodies cabinet was a cabinet that sat in the
  Counterterrorism staff office of the Diplomatic Security Service
  agency within the Dept. of State in 1986.

  "Whenever I get a free moment, I've come to enjoy launching
  little fishing expeditions to see where all the bodies are
  buried. I'll open a drawer at random and poke through the files
  until I find something interesting. Once, I even looked to see
  if we had a file on alien contact at Roswell, New Mexico. There
  wasn't anything on it. Given all the other stuff that's in
  those cabinets, I've concluded that if it is not there, it
  didn't happen."

  This is the file cabinet that contains files on the two Kennedy
  brothers assassinations that have never been exposed to the
  light of public reading.
  Interesting, yes. Who knew that a member of the Dept. of State
  would be interested in Roswell and search very private/deeply
  classified files not available to the public.

  KK

                                            Re: US Dept. of State Dead Bodies Cabinet
  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Sun, 6 Sep 2009 17:48:39 -0400
  Archived: Mon, 07 Sep 2009 08:16:33 -0400
  Subject: Re: US Dept. of State Dead Bodies Cabinet

  >From: Kathy Kasten <catraja.nul>
  >To: post to ufoupdates <post.nul>
  >Date: Sun, 6 Sep 2009 19:21:26 +0000
  >Subject: US Dept. of State Dead Bodies Cabinet
  >From the book Ghost, Confessions Of A Counterterrorism Agent, by
  >Fred Burton:
  >Chapter: Human Poker Chips, page 67.
  >The dead bodies cabinet was a cabinet that sat in the
  >Counterterrorism staff office of the Diplomatic Security Service
  >agency within the Dept. of State in 1986.

  >"Whenever I get a free moment, I've come to enjoy launching
  >little fishing expeditions to see where all the bodies are
  >buried. I'll open a drawer at random and poke through the files
  >until I find something interesting. Once, I even looked to see
  >if we had a file on alien contact at Roswell, New Mexico. There
  >wasn't anything on it. Given all the other stuff that's in
  >those cabinets, I've concluded that if it is not there, it
  >didn't happen."

  >This is the file cabinet that contains files on the two Kennedy
  >brothers' assassinations that have never been exposed to the
  >light of public reading.

  Given what we know about the Kennedy assassinations (in JFK's
  case, more than any murder in history), it's unlikely that the
  files contain much likely to interest anyone but historians.
  More often than not, secrecy remains only because governments
  mindlessly hide documents long past their time, one consequence
  of which is the fueling of conspiracy theories. Which I should
  think defeats the purpose, but who says bureaucratic humans are
  rational animals?
  One conceivable reading of Burton's non-discovery is that the
  Roswell files, being far more significant than those related to
  the Kennedys et al., are housed nowhere near those of less
  explosive significance.
  Then again, who knows? I say that as a Roswell agnostic who's as
  in the dark as anyone. Until conclusive answers are known and
  documented, I'm just spinning wool out of thin air like
  everybody else.
  Thanks, in any case, for the item, Kathy. It's most interesting.

  Jerry Clark

http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m07-006.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:41]
Plan 9 Actor Proud Of Worst Movie




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 8

                                                Plan 9 Actor Proud Of Worst Movie
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Mon, 7 Sep 2009 18:03:04 EDT
  Archived: Tue, 08 Sep 2009 07:27:54 -0400
  Subject: Plan 9 Actor Proud Of Worst Movie

  http://www.wtop.com/?nid=25&sid=1756927

  Plan 9 From Outer Space has long been accepted as the worst
  movie ever made. I would debate that but the experts say it is
  so it must be so.
  This particular movie has several meanings for me because it was
  written and directed by Ed Wood Jr., also voted as the worst
  director of all time. In this news tidbit, actor Conrad Brooks
  who played Jamie the cop in the classic B movie talks about the
  flick.
  I remember seeing this movie playing at the theatres in my
  hometown and on TV. Sometimes at the same time. In the Hudson
  Valley, now famous for its UFO sightings and last field
  investigation of J. Allen Hynek, Ed Wood Jr. was famous because
  he was from the Hudson Valley, Poughkeepsie, NY in fact.
  People were terrified of this movie back home and it wasn't
  until the Hudson Valley UFO sightings increased did this movie
  rise again. We'd all heard stories about UFOs over graveyards,
  strange beings walking the roads and terrorizing residents. One
  famous pic had a silver suited fellow walking the roads of Hyde
  Park, NY.
  I sometimes wonder what the heck was on Ed Wood Jr.'s mind and
  how he came up with this wild story but I'm figuring it was
  hometown experience that added to it.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com/blog

  Hi all,
  I am not taking the author's remarks immediately at face value.
  The author's puzzling statement could also have been any of the
  following options:
  - a snipe at the UFO communities (with Roswell as its most
  popularly known case) make them look silly
  - a tongue in cheek jest aimed at no-one, but his peculiar taste
  of humor
  - inflation of the author's importance
  - the as yet little understood 'Corso-effect'
  - an injoke meant to be understood amongst peers
  - a general joke for a popular readership
  (Roswell, Kennedy, what icons of the fringe are more or equally
  known, except for bigfoot and Nessie?)
  Sincere regards,
  Theo

PRG Press Release - 9/8/09 - PRG Speaking Tour




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 8

                                     PRG Press Release - 9/8/09 - PRG Speaking Tour
  From: Stepehn G Bassett <PRG.nul>
  Date: Tue, 08 Sep 2009 06:12:43 -0400
  Archived: Tue, 08 Sep 2009 08:35:00 -0400
  Subject: PRG Press Release - 9/8/09 - PRG Speaking Tour


  PRG
  Paradigm Research Group
  Press Release - September 8, 2009
  PRG Europe Speaking Tour
  Washington, DC - PRG executive director Stephen Bassett will
  leave on Thursday, September 10 for a forty day speaking tour of
  Europe to include seventeen stops in ten countries. The tour
  begins at the Beyond Knowledge Conference in Liverpool, UK.
  Advocacy for a political resolution to the UFO/ET issue, which
  began to coalesce in the United States in the early 1990's, has
  now become an international "Disclosure Movement" seeking to end
  an official embargo of the truth regarding an extraterrestrial
  presence engaging the human race. Nowhere is this movement
  growing faster than in Europe.
  Mr. Bassett is a leading advocate for ending this truth embargo.
  Paradigm Research Group was founded in 1996 exclusively for this
  purpose and was the first organization to register with the
  United States Congress to lobby the question. PRG's political
  action committee X-PPAC is the first PAC to address the UFO/ET
  issue. In 2002 Bassett became the second person (after Jimmy
  Carter) to bring the UFO/ET subject into a federal election
  campaign as a balloted candidate.
  In 2004 PRG produced the first conference centered on the
  politics surrounding the UFO/ET phenomenon - exopolitics. The
  sixth X-Conference is scheduled for April 16-18, 2010 near
  Washington, DC. In 2008 PRG founded the Exopolitics World
  Network (EWN) and Exopolitics United States Network (EUSN) to
  help facilitate the expansion of the disclosure movement. EWN
  member Exopolitics United Kingdom is a cosponsor of the
  Liverpool Beyond Knowledge Conference.
  PRG's advocacy work has been covered extensively by US and
  foreign media.
  On the tour Mr. Bassett hopes to gain a greater understanding of
  how Europeans view the UFO/ET issue and the disclosure process.
  He will brief informal and formal groups on the pending collapse
  of the truth embargo, possibly in 2009, work to bring new
  countries into the EWN and seek new support for the ongoing
  advocacy initiatives.
  The tour schedule is as follows: UK: Liverpool (Sept 11-14),
  Bath (Sept 14-16), Devon (Sept. 16-18), London (Sept 18-21),
  Birmingham (Sept 21), London (Sept 22-24); Helsinki (Sept 24-
   28); Stockholm (Sept 28-30); Oslo (Sept 30-Oct. 2); Berlin (Oct
  2-4); Copenhagen (Oct 4-8); Brussels (Oct 8-10); Hulst,
  Amsterdam (Oct 10-12); Zurich (Oct 12-14); France: Rennes (Oct
  14-16); Saint-Pierre-en-Port (Oct 16-18); Paris (Oct 18-20).
  More information about the tour schedule including events,
  websites and contacts can be found at:

  Subject: Fighter Pilot Flies Alongside UFO

  In today's 'The Sun' toilet paper, a video clip of a fighter
  aircraft apparently following a 'blob' (described by 'The Scum'
  as a 'triangular-shaped craft' which is emmiting a contrail).
  Anyone who thinks that the blob is 'Triangular' missed out on
  their Primary school geometry lessons.
  Yet again, no context for the clip, it was just 'leaked' on the
  Internet.
  My guess is a fighter chasing a target drone. Take your own
  guess at The Sc...Sun Website:
  http://tinyurl.com/nlf6qc

  Subject: Plan 9 Actor Proud Of Worst Movie

  <so it must be so.

  Greg.)
  As for me, I love the cheese-o-rama that is Plan 9.... the two
  cent production values with every variety of acting possible...
  from going through the motions to going for an Oscar.
  And the soundtrack rocks.

  Mike Woods

   simple-minded really.

  having investigated a single case or referencing the case files
  of actual UFO investigators and who are given 85% of the talk
  time on documentaries.

  valid because I just don't know anything about documentary
  production.

   >>>"Research by proclamation", "Absence of evidence is not evidence

   >>Are you fighting for "Research by proclamation", John? I know
   >>you would be almost speechless without it. "Absence of evidence
   >>is not evidence of absence" did not come from any of those in
   >>the UFO field. It is an old science adage - believe some British
   >>scientist from the 19th century.
   >>"Cosmic Watergate" doesn't match up with the others and is used
   >>by Stan Friedman in his presentations. He is one person not a
   >>huge, ignorant media system.
   >>Laziness, pure laziness on their part. It is used to set the
   >>tone by the writers to disassociate themselves form the article
   >>while still making a buck off of it. Cowardice, and
   >>simple-minded really.

   >I tend to agree with you Don,
   An historic moment, John.
   >all the phrases above, including
   >the ones I quote, are evidence of laziness and retreat to a
   >secure comfort zone, and suggest that the post containing them
   >is unlikely to tell me anything new.
   Imagine, though, how well the phrase, "Absence of evidence is
   not evidence of absence" fit science in the early years when
   microscopes and other measuring devises could not 'see' the
   evidence even though it was there. That hold's true even now due
   to technical limitations in physics and in other sciences.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m12-003.shtml[11/12/2011 19:26:52]
Re: The British X-Files In Full

   Suspected, but can't be proven using the Mark I eyeball. That
   same observational piece of equipment is used to find ex-solar
   planets as well with the aid of powerful telescopes. I think the
   phrase or sentence applies quite nicely to the phenomenon we
   deal with. However the 'lack of evidence' is straining my
   credibility in this scenario.
   >I think I might also add 'skeptibunker' and 'klasskurtsian',
   >althou naturally I exclude our old friend 'pelicanist' from any
   >such procession of the damned!
   How often do you see this in the daily papers though? Can you
   match the number of 'space alien' and 'alien space craft' and
   'Mulder and Scully" jibes to those you have included above? I
   think not because in some ways the reporters themselves could
   lay claim to those hard earned titles; not from informed
   investigation but rather a self imposed ignorance. In this sense
   it is 'proclamation by opinion'.

   Don Ledger

Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 13

                                                    Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop
  From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  Date: Sat, 12 Sep 2009 00:16:09 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 13 Sep 2009 09:48:51 -0400
  Subject: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop

  The sceptics seem to have decided that if you can't beat 'em,
  join 'em and are staging their very own UFO workshop!
  The Committee for Scientific Inquiry is calling its seminar on
  Oct 9 - 11, 2009 - UFOs: The Space Age Mythology .
  The event's blurb reads: "For over 60 years the interpretation
  of “Things” in the sky has led to mythology about “Aliens” and
  their “Spacecraft.” These myths are fused with images from
  science fiction and enhanced by spiritual mysticism. Anomalous
  events in the sky are enlarged by anecdotal stories and
  conspiracy theories of “Space Gods” coming to earth for either
  salvation or destruction. Can “Lights in the Sky” really save us
  from the modern world? This workshop will consider these and
  other questions about the role of science and skepticism in
  evaluating extraordinary claims."
  The workshop features James McGaha the man who showed so little
  knowledge on the subject on the Larry King Show that I'm
  surprised he's not too embarrassed ever to show his face in
  public again!
  Also speaking at the conference held in Tucson, Arizona will be
  David Morrison, Robert Sheaffer and Dave Thomas.
  Topics being covered include The Betty Hill Story, Roswell,
  Mexican UFO Wave, Physical Evidence, Moon and Mars Artifacts,
  Aztec/Socorro and Aliens Among Us

  Full details at:
  http://ufocon.org/

  From: Viktor Glubic <Diverge247.nul>
  Date: Mon, 14 Sep 2009 11:53:19 EDT
  Archived: Mon, 14 Sep 2009 15:17:31 -0400
  Subject: Re: News Links 12-09-09 - UFO over AZ

  >News Links 12-09-09
  >>From 'The Norm':
  >12-Year-Old Snaps Photo Of UFO Over Arizona Neighborhood
  http://tinyurl.com/rdqasf
  Case 19210
  This photo analysis, if representative of the MUFON report, does
  not show anything unexpected on the normal pixel process area in
  a low resolution image - the normal contrast between objects in
  an image is mathematically redistributed in processing along the
  equalized (square) linear transition boundaries as shown. If
  this was a pasted image placed over the window, then so too is
  the tree on the left.
  This reveals more of the image and has been converted to B&W -
  here we can see the similarity between the UFO (U) and the tree
  (T) boundaries.
  http://tinyurl.com/noyzun
  If faked, it is not, for the reason stated in the article. I'm
  not aware if higher resolution images are available since I
  downloaded this particular one from their actual site.

  VG

  >From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>

  If I were to speak at the skeptics workshop I would tell them
  about Phil Klass' 1983 book, UFOS: The Public Deceived and I
  would point out how, with that book, Phil 'deceived' the public.
  In that book Phil presented and discussed the Val Johnson police
  car damage case in Warren, Minnesota in Aug 1980. He gave a good
  recitation of the case and 'proved' that it was either a result
  of mean, nasty ETs that damaged the car as they flew past - hit
  the car with a hammer and bent the antennas - or else Johnson
  did it himself as a practical joke.
  He did not point out that Johnson was never charged with
  damaging public property and the people who worked with him
  always believed his story, which included a strange effect on
  his watch and police car clock - 15 minutes off after the event.
  Phil left it up to the reader to decide, joke/hoax or "real
  thing." By not pointing out how unlikely the hoax explanation he
  deceived the public.

  >Subject: Re: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop
  >If I were to speak at the skeptics workshop I would tell them
  >about Phil Klass' 1983 book, UFOS: The Public Deceived and I
  >would point out how, with that book, Phil 'deceived' the public.
  >In that book Phil presented and discussed the Val Johnson police
  >car damage case in Warren, Minnesota in Aug 1980. He gave a good >
  >recitation of the case and 'proved' that it was either a result
  >of mean, nasty ETs that damaged the car as they flew past - hit
  >the car with a hammer and bent the antennas - or else Johnson
  >did it himself as a practical joke.
  I don't agree with Klass that there were only two possible
  explanations.
  It is posssible that Johnson was attacked by some person or
  persons who damaged his car. As he was apprently rendered
  unconscious for a time, it is possible that his memories of the
  incident were not entirely accurate. Police officers do
  sometimes inevitably make enemies in the course of their work.
  I am only guessing of course, but I suspect that we have never
  been told the full details of this incident.
  Also, I find it hard to imagine why the occupants or controllers
  of UFOs would want to vandalise police cars.

  John Harney

  justification!

  clattering concussively if effortlessly off the tongue and
  palate like a big porcelain urn of live snakes dropped in the
  CSI Commissary.
  It is in no way "clumsy". Tsk and Sniff! It is a word delivering
  justified blows on many well deserved levels, Sir! Tsk I say!
  "Flaming Fertilizer", a real delight of alliteration used as it

                                                   Re: Klass & Police Car Damage
  From: Jerome Clark <jkclark.nul>
  Date: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 08:37:25 -0400
  Archived: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 11:40:42 -0400
  Subject: Re: Klass & Police Car Damage

  >From: John Harney <magonia.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Wed, 16 Sep 2009 21:18:35 +0100
  >Subject: Klass & Police Car Damage [was: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop]
  >>From: Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>
  >>To: post.nul
  >>Date: Wed, 16 Sep 2009 14:06:20 -0400
  >>Subject: Re: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop
  >>If I were to speak at the skeptics workshop I would tell them
  >>about Phil Klass' 1983 book, UFOS: The Public Deceived and I
  >>would point out how, with that book, Phil 'deceived' the public.
  >>In that book Phil presented and discussed the Val Johnson police
  >>car damage case in Warren, Minnesota in Aug 1980. He gave a good >
  >>recitation of the case and 'proved' that it was either a result
  >>of mean, nasty ETs that damaged the car as they flew past - hit
  >>the car with a hammer and bent the antennas - or else Johnson
  >>did it himself as a practical joke.
  >I don't agree with Klass that there were only two possible
  >explanations.
  >It is posssible that Johnson was attacked by some person or
  >persons who damaged his car. As he was apprently rendered
  >unconscious for a time, it is possible that his memories of the
  >incident were not entirely accurate. Police officers do
  >sometimes inevitably make enemies in the course of their work.
  >I am only guessing of course, but I suspect that we have never
  >been told the full details of this incident.
  This is speculative, unfalsifiable, and meaningless. It is,
  however, typical. It's one reason I have never been attracted to
  the debunking ideology. When the facts won't do - and the facts
  are hard to refute - conjure up some "truth" that's out there
  but, in some sinister fashion, never to be recovered, to be
  hidden so effectively that not even a hint of it ever comes to
  light. (Longtime readers may recall comparable convolutions in
  this space regarding the Trindade photographs.) When ufologists
  talk that way - as all of us know except those whose failed
  sense of irony triggers amnesia whenever necessary - people like
  John Harney ridicule them as conspiracy theorists.
  In reality, the case was investigated almost immediately after
  its occurrence by no less than Allan Hendry of CUFOS, who did a
  fine job and whom Magonians quote adoringly when he's saying
  what they want to hear. No evidence of a hoax emerged, and none
  has since.* To every available indication, short of the solution
  of the UFO mystery itself, we know everything relevant that
  there is to know.
  It is worth noting that encounters with generally similar
  features occurred in the area in the same general time frame.
  (Johnson's encounter occurred in rural northwestern Minnesota.)
  One took place in southeastern South Dakota to the south two
  days later. None were so dramatic as Johnson's, but they did
  indicate the presence of the sort of UFO type and associated


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m17-005.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:02]
Re: Klass & Police Car Damage

  activity (namely a brilliant light source barreling down a rural
  highway toward a moving automobile) that Johnson described. Or
  maybe Johnson and associates orchestrated those, too.
  In the spirit of one who hurls mud at a wall, Klass advanced the
  argument that Johnson's case must be a hoax because nobody had
  ever reported anything like it. While researching The UFO
  Encyclopedia (see p. 611, second edition), I found a strikingly
  comparable incident, entirely overlooked by ufologists and
  everybody else, in an obscure small-town Minnesota paper from
  May 1961. Unfortunately, this one - unlike Johnson's - had no
  Allan Hendry to investigate it.
  >Also, I find it hard to imagine why the occupants or
  >controllers of UFOs would want to vandalise police cars.
  Oh, I don't believe for a moment that you have any problem with
  an active imagination, John. But I confess I, whose imaginative
  powers clearly are inferior to yours, have no idea how or why
  you claim to know that "the occupants or controllers" intended
  their action as vandalism or regarded it as such. Either you
  read alien minds, or you just made that up.
  Jerry Clark

  *John knows nothing of small-town Minnesota life, of course. At
  one time Magonians went to some length, believe it or not, to
  ridicule the very concept, as readers of that august journal
  will recall. But probably only those of us who do know something
  about it from long experience (in my case, more than 37 years)
  will appreciate the extravagant humor inherent in the notion
  that an operation so sophisticated, intricate, and public as
  John's imagined something or other (Project X?) will be able to
  conceal its secrets for more than, say, a few weeks. And then
  only if it's had an unusually successful run and no one has
  bothered to examine the claim very carefully. John proposes
  Project X in order to shrink this ostensibly puzzling, richly
  documented CE2 into something that will no longer frighten small
  children and pelicanists.

Majestic-12 Papers Authored By Stanton Friedman

                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 17

                                    Majestic-12 Papers Authored By Stanton Friedman
  From: Greg Taylor <greg.nul>
  Date: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 23:51:51 +1000
  Archived: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 11:43:29 -0400
  Subject: Majestic-12 Papers Authored By Stanton Friedman

  So says Brian Dunning anyhow. And he's a skeptic, so it must be
  true.
  http://skepticblog.org/2009/09/10/who-wrote-the-majestic-12-papers/
  "The late Philip Klass, perhaps the best known of the skeptical
  UFO investigators, once tried to flush out the author.
  One of the papers, known as the Cutler/Twining Memo, was in a
  certain unusual typeface, and Klass reasoned that whoever wrote
  it probably had authentic government documents from the same
  period to use as a style guide. He offered a cash reward for any
  extant examples.
  Who sprang forward with handy documents to claim the prize? None
  other than Stanton Friedman. Klass paid him $1000 (probably
  considering it a bargain), and Friedman's reputation was
  permanently stained as a result.
  Tip to paranormal hoaxers hoping to make a career out of it:
  Don't take the bait if you don't want to be hooked."

  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>

  >From: Greg Taylor <greg.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 23:51:51 +1000
  >Subject: Majestic-12 Papers Authored By Stanton Friedman
  >So says Brian Dunning anyhow. And he's a skeptic, so it must be
  >true.
  >http://skepticblog.org/2009/09/10/who-wrote-the-majestic-12-papers/
  >"The late Philip Klass, perhaps the best known of the skeptical
  >UFO investigators, once tried to flush out the author.
  >One of the papers, known as the Cutler/Twining Memo, was in a
  >certain unusual typeface, and Klass reasoned that whoever wrote
  >it probably had authentic government documents from the same
  >period to use as a style guide. He offered a cash reward for any
  >extant examples.
  >Who sprang forward with handy documents to claim the prize? None
  >other than Stanton Friedman. Klass paid him $1000 (probably
  >considering it a bargain), and Friedman's reputation was
  >permanently stained as a result.
  >Tip to paranormal hoaxers hoping to make a career out of it:
  >Don't take the bait if you don't want to be hooked."
  Nice theory, but if Mr. Friedman had been perpetrating a hoax why
  would he have willfully offered up all his MJ-12 and Twining
  memo documents to authorization by the foremost authority on
  documentation authorization? We covered this on UFO Updates
  months ago.
  It just so happens a classmate of mine is one of the foremost
  authorities on document authorization and I presented his
  contact info right here on this list and Mr. Friedman was the
  first to offer up his documents.
  Doesn't seem to me that a hoaxster would leap into the lion's
  den with both feet.
  My pal Bart is back in the U.S. and I'm sure if there are
  documents that qualify for authentication he'll be more than
  happy to look at them as well as pass them on to other experts
  in the field.

  Best,
  Greg

                                                 Re: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop
  From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  Date: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 13:45:01 -0300
  Archived: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 15:45:04 -0400
  Subject: Re: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop

  >From:Bruce Maccabee <brumac.nul>
  >To: post.nul
  >Date: Wed, 16 Sep 2009 14:06:20 -0400
  >Subject: Re: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop
  >>From: Don Ledger <dledger.nul>
  >>To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >>Date: Wed, 16 Sep 2009 14:02:30 -0300
  >>Subject: Re: Sceptics Stage UFO Workshop
  >If I were to speak at the skeptics workshop I would tell them
  >about Phil Klass' 1983 book, UFOS: The Public Deceived and I
  >would point out how, with that book, Phil 'deceived' the public.
  >In that book Phil presented and discussed the Val Johnson police
  >car damage case in Warren, Minnesota in Aug 1980. He gave a good
  >recitation of the case and 'proved' that it was either a result
  >of mean, nasty ETs that damaged the car as they flew past - hit
  >the car with a hammer and bent the antennas - or else Johnson
  >did it himself as a practical joke.
  >He did not point out that Johnson was never charged with
  >damaging public property and the people who worked with him
  >always believed his story, which included a strange effect on
  >his watch and police car clock - 15 minutes off after the event.
  >Phil left it up to the reader to decide, joke/hoax or "real
  >thing." By not pointing out how unlikely the hoax explanation he
  >deceived the public.
  I found that book at a used book store in Ottawa in 1999. I read
  it but found it childish, to be honest. Everything was
  over-simplified with no proof of his having solved anything
  though his thinking seemed to be that he was. He seemed to be
  relying on public ignorance to buoy his burdens of proof - which
  were silly. I gave it away.
  The main difference I found in his book as opposed to the
  investigative side was that where the mainstream offered up
  cases which had no explanation and it was left to the reader to
  make a decision as to whether they thought the case was worth
  exploring; Klass came up with explanations which clearly did not
  fit the evidence presented.
  When that didn't work he made light of it and in effect dared
  the reader to disagree under penalty of their being
  un-enlightened; a ploy still used today by lazy proclaimers on
  the debunking side.

  Don ledger
