  John, there is no evidence to support your interperation of the
  Val Johson incident (that he was attacked by unknown
  individuals). Val Johson's description of the light, the damage
  to the car,and the physiological injuries sustined by Val
  Johnson all negate your interperation.
  Val Johson's description of the light was detailed and certainly
  not vague:
  =E2=80=9CI noticed a very bright, brilliant light, 8 to 12 inches in
  diameter, 3 to 4 feet off the ground. The edges were very
  defined... (interview with G Wescott 1979)
  The light also approached Val Johnson at an extreme rate (high
  velocity), indeed the light was approximately 2.5km away from
  Val Johnson, when it =E2=80=9Cinstantaneously=E2=80=9D approached the car.
  What conventional stimulus could perform such a feat?



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m18-001.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:06]
Re: Klass & Police Car Damage

  The damage to the car was extensive and has many peculiar
  features.. In particular the conclusion derived by Meridian
  French, a windshield expert from Ford, who examined the damage
  is most illuminating:
  =E2=80=9CEven after several days of reflection on the crack patterns
  and apparent sequence of fractures, I still have no explanation
  for what seem to be inward and outward forces acting almost
  simultaneously. I can only [conclude].... that all cracks were
  from mechanical forces of unknown origin=E2=80=9D
  Thus if John=E2=80=99s interpretation is correct, at least two
  individuals must have been involved in the =E2=80=9Cattack=E2=80=9D. One
  would have been required to enter the car and damage the
  windshield, whilst the other individual damaged the windshield
  from the outside (at the same time).
  This seems highly unlikely and does not deserve further comment.
  The damage to the antennas, the failure of the engine to charge
  and other aspects of the damage are also puzzling and are
  difficult to reconcile, with John's interpretation.
  The physical injuries sustained by Val Johnson, particularly the
  =E2=80=9Cburning of the eyes=E2=80=9D are also inconsistent with an attack.=
  Dr W.A Pinsonneault, who examined Val Johnson compared the eye
  damage to=E2=80=9Cmild welder burns=E2=80=9D.
  If one suggests that a =E2=80=9Chigh powered beam=E2=80=9D was
  focused/concentrated upon Val Johnson by individuals unknown,
  then this scenario fails to explain the extensive damage to the
  car.
  Finally (and one could go on) there is the issue of Val
  Johnson's watch and the car clock, both of which were 14 minutes
  slow ( I suppose the individuals who =E2=80=9Cattacked=E2=80=9D Val Johnson=
  could have re-wound his watch and re-set the clock!). It is
  important to note that Val Johnson checked the time with
  dispatch at 7pm (as he always did).
  I have absolutely no idea of what Val Johnson encountered. The
  evidence, however does not support John's intereperation.

  Best
  Lee Dines

  Majestic 12.

  memo.

  never been. He told many about challenging me and nobody about
  paying me. He had _not_ made a general offer fishing for a sucker.
  There is much more to the story as described in TOP SECRET/MAJIC
  and other documents. His check is also in Flying Saucers And
  Science.

  phony. So what?

  for good anti-MJ-12 arguments with which I have not yet dealt.
  Incidentally the CT memo is at the National Archives. Many have
  falsely claimed that TOP SECRET Restricted was never used...
  wrong again... or that since Cutler was out of the country, he
  could not have written it.
  Of course it isn't signed and George Elsey, who worked for
  Truman the entire time HST was in the White House, agreed that
  James Lay almost certainly would have prepared it, etc., etc., etc.

  Stan Friedman

  they could not be taken to the Archives).

  presume that the document is still available in the file in
  which it was found, oh, so long ago! Next best, after going to
  the Archives, are the scanned slide photos which are at my web

  explanation of the flashing light triangle which appears in the
  last section of the New Zealand color movie film of Dec. 31,
  1978.

  result of light from the upper (on top of the airplane) red
  rotating beacon that was reflected from the nearest (to the
  fuselage) rotating propellor blade at the right side of the
  airplane.

  why this explanation made no sense. However, that didn't stop
  him from publishing his "explanation." He did mention that I
  disagreed with him but didn't publish the reason why. If he had
  published the reason why the reader would have immediately seen
  through his "explanation."

  from the door frame, and it seems to fit the shape of the
  mirror. But the mirror is plastic, and the pillar is thick
  steel. That's like dropping an egg in a skillet and seeing the
  egg dent the pan."
  I commented then that plastic denting steel might be possible if
  the impact was accompanied by a field that reduced the binding
  force in the steel so that it was malleable at the time of the
  impact.
  Wilbert Smith was told back in the 1950s by "the boys upstairs"
  that a reduction in binding force was responsible for the
  destruction of aircraft that got too close to alien saucer
  craft. The same kind of effect might now be present in other
  current technologies, black or otherwise.
  Some of the mystery about the physical evidence in the Val
  Johnson case is removed if we accept that some kind of pressure
  wave hit the car which was accompanied by a field that softened
  metal.
  1) The bridegework in his mouth was broken at the gums, although
  it was otherwise intact. There was no pain or swelling in his
  mouth. This is no surprise if the metal softened but fractured
  only at the stress points.
  2) Spring loaded radio antennae were bent well away from the
  anchor point on the car. Insects were still attached, suggesting
  that the bending force was not that great. Again, if the
  softening of the antenna metal were localized where the bend
  occurred, the accompanying pressure wave would bend it like one
  of Uri Geller's spoons.
  3) The car clock and driver's watch were both 14 minutes slow
  after the impact. According to the information Wilbert Smith
  received, the region of reduced molecular binding force around
  the saucer is associated with a modified tempic field gradient,
  which is associated with the rate of time flow. This is in
  accord with the observed change in Johnson's clock time.
  So there is a degree of consistency among these kinds of cases
  given the right hypothesis, and this consistency, in turn,
  supports the hypothesis.

  William

  similar rare natural phenomenon. But, then, the generally agreed
  facts of the case make it seem very unlikely.

  psychological explanation seems appropriate, but the physical
  evidence rules it out in this case. It is particularly
  interesting because no one has been able to suggest a plausible
  explanation. I agree that Klass's attempt to label it a hoax was
  absurd.

  >there is to know.
  I have not made any criticisms of Hendry's investigation of the case.

  >everybody else, in an obscure small-town Minnesota paper from

  >Allan Hendry to investigate it.

Brazilian M.D. Retrieved Aliens After Crash

                                              Brazilian M.D. Retrieved Aliens After Crash
  From: Eustaquio Andrea Patounas <socex.ufobras.nul>
  Date: Fri, 18 Sep 2009 17:47:44 -0300
  Archived: Fri, 18 Sep 2009 19:06:09 -0400
  Subject: Brazilian M.D. Retrieved Aliens After Crash

  Brazilian M.D. Swears He Has Retrieved Aliens After A Craft
  Crash
  This was presented today on a morning news broadcast. The
  witness, not really a M.D. but a paramedic with a driver, both
  in an ambulance, in a night routine patrol,on September 25
  2005,suddenly saw a light coming from a yard beside of the road,
  in the State of S=E3o Paulo. Thinking it was an usual car accident
  they entered the bushes. To their dismay, it was a crashed
  spherical craft, almost destroyed, having two occupants, one
  alive and very hurt and the other dead and presenting signs of
  multiple fractures, in short, a multi-lesion lost case. The
  paramedic and the driver tried to rescue the survivor, but
  although speaking a strange language, somehow, they experienced
  to begin to understand the only humanoid alive. He said to the
  rescuers that in fact they were time time- travelers, e.g. they
  were from the future but from Earth, on a mission to pick
  genetic material they were missing on their time dimension. Next
  the alien oriented the paramedic to use something taken from the
  craft interior a sort of icy material, which had the astonishing
  propriety of stop blood bleeding, clotting it quickly.During
  rescue, the paramedic noticed that some things looked similar to
  humans like heartbeats, for instance.
  Naturally rescuers warnedd their hospital (or authorities)- not
  sre- and a vehicle came and both aliens, corpse and survivor,
  were transfered into it, and they were told that their
  destination was the University of Campinas, the famous UNICAMP.
  The witness agreed to tell his story to the reporter (Eduad
  P=F3lo-Rede TV) on the back as to the camera, black shirt with
  long sleeves, bald, with some blond grizzly hair, and distorted
  voice. According to him, after some time they were called by
  their superiors to shout their mouths, but even so he was lately
  fired with his companion, and now works in another job, in a
  very good hospital of the town. Said it changed entirely his
  concept about Science, religion. Now uses to spend free times
  looking for UFO concerned material on Internet,he, that
  previously simply was skeptical.
  The Rede TV network is not among of the "big ones" in Brazil,
  not very serous, but their news department seems good. Sometimes
  when I can't see the night news earlier, on the "big ones"
  (Globo. Record, Bandeirantes)their night news broadcast comes
  after the "big ones" and they're not bad.
  This story has a curious point: this is the 2nd time UNICAMP is
  pointed as te institution to where alien corpses were taken to.
  It happened during the famous Varginha alien retrieval. As one
  could expect, not a word, all kind of denials, but again?
  About the "time travelling" "revelation", once again we should
  ALWAYS remember what the late John A. Keel (passed away last
  June 3 - http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Keel) puts in his UFO
  Trojan Horse - Putnan - 1970-NY (The Contactee Hoax-p.212-214)
  and also Jacques Valee on his The Invisible College (The
  phenomenon negates itself) and especially The Six Social
  Consequences in Messengers of Deception.

  hesitate to say what plainly seems an idiocy. In short, impressed
  (and fooled) by someone else's "superiority" she chose to accept
  the "superior knowledge".,n otherwiords a lie... from the
  aliens. If a person is not overtly insane, she won't do that and
  this gets to reinforce this Brazilian paramedic's report even
  more. He kept himself silent for four years and many people
  around the world do it FOREVER.

  From: Greg Boone<Evolbaby.nul>>
  Date: Thu, 17 Sep 2009 11:12:22 EDT

  Subject: New "Montauk Monster" Spotted In Panama

  http://tinyurl.com/lbjce2
  The only way this story is UFO related is in the data that
  states this creature was beaten to death by fearful residents
  that thought it was going to harm them. It may be a joke but the
  story is similar to past stories about how we humans react to
  other life.
  The same story come from that ever growing Alien Baby report
  that journalist Jaime Maussan has been covering for years. ( Let
  us note that Jaime hasn't said it's an alien, the press put that
  tag on that creature ). That when the creature was confronted by
  humans it was killed out of fear.
  How will humanity learn of other life forms if the first thing
  we do is kill them? These two incidents are from present time
  events, but how many have occured in the past? We've heard
  rumors that have stated that even the beings from the Roswell
  incident might have met a fearsome doom at the hands of
  frightened military or townsfolk.
  Just a few months ago a report about our Mars Rovers that they
  may have accidently killed microbial life while doing their
  experiments to look for life. Are we humans all thumbs and
  worse? Maybe we don't need to meet other species as I've stated
  in many an essay and email past. We can't even live next door to
  one another if we have different hair color or skin color and we
  expect that one day we'd be able to meet and interract with
  beings from other worlds?
  On our interstellar report card it probably has an F next to the
  cartoon of the stick figure kids labeled plays_well_with_others.

  Best,
  Greg

Re: UFOs Return To Wycliffe Wells




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 20

                                                Re: UFOs Return To Wycliffe Wells
  From: Terry Colvin <fortean1.nul>
  Date: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 12:49:39 +0700 (GMT+07:00)
  Archived: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 11:20:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: UFOs Return To Wycliffe Wells

  [forteana] Re: UFOs Return to Wycliffe Wells
  Date: Sep 20, 2009 3:19 AM
  Whycliffe Wells revisited, huh?
  You hear so many mixed accounts about UFO sightings that I feel
  it is incumbent on us to go out there and talk to the actual
  witnesses, talk to others in the vicinity - especially sceptics
  - and read what media accounts there are about the phenomenon.
  So when there was a flurry of sightings reported at WW in 1995 I
  went with my sons out to the place to see what was really going
  on. I never found out.
  True, I had a bizarre telepathic experience on the way out
  there, having never had voices in my head in my adult life. A
  thundering voice while I was driving at night told me in garbled
  speak they were friendly and that they had gifts for me. I did
  not stop, except shortly afterwards to jot down what I heard and
  tell my sons who had been asleep. There was a joint USA-Oz
  military exercise going on in the Northern Territory at the time
  and I later learned the US military did have something they
  called Voice-of-God technology. Certainly something exterior to
  my skull communicated to me. If something like that spoke into
  Abraham and Moses' heads I can understand why they were
  converted to Judaism. The phenomenon certainly exploited
  material already there in the brain, and that is all I will now
  say on that subject.
  And I found at Tennant Creek library plenty of newspaper reports
  by people of sometimes very scary encounters with hovering disc-
  shaped craft. Later in the UFO literature there were further
  reports of similar events in other states in Australia. One
  striking case was near Maitland, N.S.W. where a craft was
  observed beaming radiation into the salt water below and sucking
  up the steam, a great way to collect pure water it would seem.
  At WW itself in mid-1995 I did speak to proprieter Lou Farkas
  and he gave me descriptions of repeat visits by UFOs to his
  glorified caravan park, but he had no photos to show me. I found
  no guests there that had seen anything, and I hung around about
  a week and saw nothing, One staff member told me he had seen a
  hovering object but he was not especially credible. Meanwhile
  several very relieable staff members told me no UFOs had been
  seen at all!
  What I did find of interest was that Lou Farkas' Chinese wife
  had set up a shrine in the shop with a giant toy ape at its
  centre. But nobody was forthcoming about that was about. It was
  not easy living out there with flies that insisted on injecting
  eggs into our tear ducts (maggots eventually drop down into your
  throat) and the constant threat of running out of water in a
  region with an incredibly low population density.
  Most tourists out there hurtle through in posh cars and stop at
  tourist parks like WW in air-conditioned comfort and they were
  hardly likely to see anything. What we did was go and camp in
  the scattered bush across the road from WW where the aborigines
  came and went. They were friendly and did not bother us. Several


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m20-004.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:16]
Re: UFOs Return To Wycliffe Wells

  were alarmed that I had a young blond son there and warned that
  he was likely to be abducted by little men who live in the trees
  and the rocks and who would teach him strange skills.
  Perhaps most damning of all was that the aborigines I met had
  seen no UFOs, but they did tell me they knew about them, and
  that they came around to fetch water.
  Then one day I met one of them again who excitedly told me he
  had seen one! It was going slow and had a flashing green light,
  he told me. Then one morning not far north of WW my sons and I
  saw a craft slow and silent as a dirigible go right overhead.
  But I later identified that as a YF-22 advanced tactical
  fighter, perhaps on the last leg of a suborbital flight from
  continental USA - dare I say from Area 51. Fuckin impressive
  technology!!!
  I did eventually work out where the fair dinkum UFOs were
  emanating from, and I think Lou Farkas was being at least partly
  truthful, though I was left suspecting that he was promoting the
  UFO thing for the benefit of his own business.
  If the pattern runs true then over the next year or so there
  will be many more reports of UFO activity with craft following
  motor vehicles and hovering over water.
  Lawrie Williams
  -----
  UFOs Return to Wycliffe Wells
      Posted by: "Peter Darben"
      Date: Fri Sep 18, 2009 5:44 pm ((PDT))
  (from The NT News (Darwin) 19.09.09)
  http://www.ntnews.com.au/article/2009/09/19/86031_ntnews.html
  UFOs Return To Happy NT Hunting Ground
  Matt Cunningham
  Unidentified flying objects have returned to a famous Territory
  hot spot.
  Workers and tourists at the Wycliffe Well Holiday Park reported
  seeing strange lights hovering in the night sky last week.
  Park employee Julie Zanker, 36, said she walked outside on
  Thursday night of last week and was amazed at what she saw.
  "We were looking at the sky and there was a white light with red
  and blue flashing lights around it," she said.
  "It was just sitting there, hovering. It definitely wasn't a
  plane or a helicopter."
  She said about eight people - staff and tourists - witnessed the
  strange lights hovering in the night sky.
  "I saw it first and then we all came out," she said. "It was
  just sitting there and then it started going up and down.
  "It was as if it knew we were watching it."
  Ms Zanker said she watched the lights for about 45 minutes
  before going to have a shower.
  But when she returned outside she said the scene was even more
  remarkable.
  "I went out for a cigarette and there were about 10 of them up
  there," she said.
  "They were all white with red and blue flashing lights."
  Ms Zanker said her group were not the only ones to witness the
  light show.
  "A guy came in the next day and I said to him 'I think I'm
  losing it'," she said.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m20-004.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:16]
Re: UFOs Return To Wycliffe Wells

  "He works for a government mob and had been out at a station. He
  said he had been outside at about 10.30pm and had seen about six
  of them."
  Wycliffe Well, about 380km north of Alice Springs, is a renowned
  UFO hot spot.
  Holiday Park owner Lew Farkas claims to have seen more than 30
  UFOs in his time.
  Ms Zanker, who has been in Wycliffe Well for about three months,
  said she had previously been a sceptic.
  "But I said to Lew the other day, 'Maybe you're not mad'," she
  said.

MoD In Bid For Alien Tech




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 20

                                                       MoD In Bid For Alien Tech
  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 13:12:36 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 11:23:00 -0400
  Subject: MoD In Bid For Alien Tech

  Source: The Sunday Times
  http://tinyurl.com/lzg7bm
  September 20, 2009
  Take Me To Your Gizmos: MoD In Bid For Alien Tech
  Jack Grimston
  The Ministry of Defence ordered an investigation of thousands of
  UFO sightings in the 1990s to examine whether alien spaceship
  technology could be exploited to build advanced engines for the
  RAF, according to a new official history.
  The book, based on the ministry's 'X-files' of thousands of
  sightings, shows that an unnamed wing commander initiated the
  project in 1993 because he believed it was wrong to assume
  extra-terrestrial craft did not exist.
  Nick Pope, who has written on UFOs and was a colleague of the
  officer at the MoD, confirmed the book's account: "I remember
  him saying he believed there was evidence of an exotic
  propulsion system and the bottom line was that if they had it,
  we sure as hell wanted it." Pope would not disclose the man's
  name.
  Official documents from the wing commander are quoted in the
  book, written by David Clarke, senior journalism lecturer at
  Sheffield Hallam University.
  The officer wrote that while most of the phenomena would have
  rational explanations, "if the sightings are of devices not of
  this earth then their purpose needs to be established as a
  matter of priority".
  He added: "There has been no apparently hostile intent and other
  possibilities are 1) military reconnaissance; 2) scientific; and
  3) tourism."
  Alien engines, if they existed, were apparently "stealthy" and
  "do not use conventional reaction propulsion systems". The wing
  commander wanted to explore possibilities for "technology
  transfer".
  The new book, The UFO Files: The Inside Story of Real-Life
  Sightings, is published by the National Archives in Kew, west
  London, and is based largely on MoD files released there. It
  traces the story of UFO sightings in Britain from the first
  decade of the 20th century.
  Through much of the past 100 years, thousands of sightings by
  people ranging from RAF pilots to police officers and children
  have been collated by the MoD who believed they could yield
  evidence of advanced foreign aircraft of which Britain had no
  knowledge.
  Some remain hard to explain, such as foo fighters, the name
  given to balls of fire that seemed to pursue RAF bombers in the
  second world war.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m20-005.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:16]
MoD In Bid For Alien Tech


  It was only in the 1990s, partly at the wing commander's
  prompting, that the MoD finally did a systematic analysis of UFO
  sightings, called Project Condign.
  Most were explained by everyday objects, such as airships,
  clouds or even chinese lanterns, being mistakenly identified as
  alien craft, or by unusual atmospheric phenomena. No evidence of
  aliens was found.
  A retired senior official at the defence intelligence staff,
  where the wing commander was employed, said: "Some people had
  bees under their bonnet... whenever UFOs were raised at the
  weekly directorate meetings it was in a fairly light-hearted and
  dismissive way."
  Clarke's book shows sightings are still frequent. In 2007 the
  MoD opened a file on an incident over the sea near Guernsey in
  which pilots of two planes saw a "sparkling yellow object shaped
  like a long, thin cigar".
  Last year the MoD's directorate of air staff logged 285 UFO
  sightings. The ministry runs a UFO hotline at RAF High Wycombe
  in Buckinghamshire which the public can telephone with details
  of sightings. The number is 01494 496 254.

Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 20

                                   Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics
  From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  Date: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 14:03:13 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 12:25:00 -0400
  Subject: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

  What annoys me most about the 'token sceptics' - the doubters
  rolled out at every TV show or news article on the paranormal -
  is that most of the time they never seem to address the topic
  directly and say something irrelevant.
  It appears to have happened on the latest report on the so-
  called Starchild skull written by Danny Penman here:
  http://tiny.cc/hPZPV
  I understand the story was to have been used in the UK Daily
  Mail but is said to have been edged out by Michael Jackson's
  death.
  If you read the piece on the above link it quotes Lloyd Pye:
  “There are at least two dozen major physiological differences
  between this skull and that of a human. We’ve done genetic tests
  on the skull. The creature’s father was not human although his
  mother certainly was".
  Now it could be that our token sceptic David Clarke said more
  than he is quoted here but assuming this was the main thrust of
  his response, it's pathetic in addressing Pye's ten years of
  testing.
  Clarke simply says "“I don’t believe that there’s any credible
  evidence that there’s been any contact between humans and
  extraterrestrials let alone interbreeding. “Stories like this
  are interesting but also quite demeaning to us as a species.
  They claim that all of the amazing achievements made by our
  ancestors in the fields of engineering, astronomy, mathematics
  and navigation came from aliens. They say that aliens gave us
  the technology to build Stonehenge and the pyramids and the
  knowledge to navigate primitive ships across the world’s oceans.
  I think that does humanity a great disservice.”
  I'm happy to listen to sceptic's point of view if they told me
  "I've looked into this issue and found Lloyd Pye is a lunatic"
  or "The genetic tests he's done are invalid and don't stand up
  to scrutiny".
  At least if he said something like that we have issues to argue
  over. But vague statements about our species being demeaned if
  they learned knowledge from the outside, are neither here nor
  there.
  Either Lloyd Pye's research stands up or it doesn't.
  It's the same with recent TV shows over UFOs. Either the
  evidence is valid or it's not. Point out exactly where it's
  wrong, sceptics - or shut up!
  I know David is on this list so maybe he can tell us where Pye's
  evidence is right or wrong and why.

  Dave Haith




http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m20-006.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:17]
Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

RAF Suspected Aliens Of Tourist Visits




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 20

                                             RAF Suspected Aliens Of Tourist Visits
  From: Joe McGonagle <joe.mcgonagle.nul>
  Date: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 13:28:49 +0100
  Archived: Sun, 20 Sep 2009 12:27:00 -0400
  Subject: RAF Suspected Aliens Of Tourist Visits

  Source: The Daily Telegraph
  http://tinyurl.com/loqw2m
  20 Sep 2009
  RAF Suspected Aliens Of Tourist Visits
  By Jasper Copping
  Britain's X Files: RAF suspected aliens of "tourist" visits to
  Earth
  A new book reveals details about UFO sightings over British
  skies after author David Clarke studied declassified Ministry of
  Defence records.
  The book gives new insights into an incident known as "Britain's
  Roswell" as well as the belief in UFOs by high ranking defence
  officials.
  Project Condign
  Documents in the files reveal that there were high level defence
  officials in the 1990s who believed UFOs could be spacecraft
  piloted by extraterrestrials who could even be conducting
  "tourist" visits to earth.
  In 1993, an RAF Wing Commander lobbied MoD officials about the
  need for a properly funded study.
  He told them: "The national security implications (of UFOs) are
  considerable. We have many reports of strange objects in the
  skies and have never investigated them."
  He added: "If the sightings are of devices not of earth then
  their purpose needs to be established as a matter of priority.
  There has been no apparently hostile intent and other
  possibilities are: (1) military reconnaissance, (2) scientific,
  (3) tourism."
  The Wing Commander, whose name is blanked out in the documents,
  said the MoD could learn from the craft.
  "If the reports are taken at face value then devices exist that
  do not use conventional reaction propulsion systems, they have a
  very wide range of speeds and are stealthy. I suggest we could
  use the technology, if it exists."
  The internal debate in the MoD came to a head in 1995, when
  documents were made public revealing that UFO reports were
  routinely copied to specialist "Defence Intelligence" branches.
  An exasperated intelligence office wrote to the UFO Desk: "I see
  no reason for continuing to deny that (Defence Intelligence) has
  an interest in UFOs.
  "However, if the association is formally made public, then the
  MoD will no doubt be pressurised to state what the intelligence
  role/interest is.


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m20-007.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:17]
RAF Suspected Aliens Of Tourist Visits


  "This could lead to disbelief and embarrassment since few people
  are likely to believe the truth that lack of funds and higher
  priorities have prevented any study of the thousands of reports
  received."
  Dr Clarke said: "Some of these officials, like the Wing
  Commander, obviously believed in some pretty weird stuff. He
  doesn't seem to have any evidence for his theories, but seems to
  have just been watching the X Files, like everyone else at the
  time.
  "These are senior officials and yet they believe some pretty
  bizarre things."
  An inquiry, Project Condign, was eventually launched in 1996,
  apparently without the knowledge of then defence secretary
  Michael Portillo. It was completed in 2000 under Geoff Hoon.
  The report found: "That (UFOs) exist is indisputable. Credited
  with the ability to hover, land, take-off, accelerate to
  exceptional velocities and vanish, they can reportedly alter
  their direction of flight suddenly and clearly can exhibit
  aerodynamic characteristics well beyond those of any known
  aircraft of missile -- either manned or unmanned."
  It went on that, although they existed, UFOs presented no threat
  to defence.
  It found that many sightings of UFOs were in fact "plasmas" of
  gas caused by charges of electricity in the atmosphere.
  The author even suggested that exposure to plasmas could cause
  responses in parts of the brain that lead to elaborate
  hallucinations that might be interpreted as supernatural
  experiences of encounters with aliens.
  The inquiry examined seven "near misses" involving RAF aircraft
  and "unexplained aerial phenomenon".
  The unnamed author concluded that "the possibility exists that a
  fatal accident might have occurred in the past" as a result of
  aircrew avoiding a UFO.
  The study recommended that pilots should make "no attempt. to
  out manoeuvre a UAP during interception".
  "Britain's Roswell"
  This occurred in the early hours of December 26, 1980, in
  Rendlesham Forest, Suffolk, near two military bases used by the
  US Air Force: RAF Woodbridge and RAF Bentwaters.
  US security personnel from the bases ventured into the forest
  after they spotted unusual lights that they feared could be a
  crashed aircraft. They reported seeing a strange glowing object
  which moved off through the forest.
  Dr Clarke said the files from the National Archives, at Kew,
  showed the authorities had missed the opportunity to fully
  investigate the incident.
  "There was clearly a missed opportunity to investigate properly
  here," he added.
  Earlier this month, Peter Turtill, 66, from Ipswich, claimed
  that he had caused the scare by burning a lorry full of
  fertiliser.
  However, his claim has been met with scorn by some ufologists.
  Dr Clarke added: "There have been other people claiming
  responsibility for whatever happened in Rendlesham Forest. There
  is so much ambiguity about the incident and that is because
  there was not enough done at the time to look into it."

  Dear List:
  I listened to Pye's interview on Coast to Coast. I have followed
  this guy's argument for years.
  What I don't understand, based on the statements made in this
  latest interview, is that he has spent $150,000 over ten years,
  but still has not done the very straight forward DNA testing.
  Doing the DNA testing a few years back would have resolved this
  issue.
  Yet, the guy is still on the air begging for money to do the DNA
  testing. This on top of the claims that he is not milking his
  position for all that it is worth.
  I would say this to Pye: the cost of $350 would have covered a
  DNA testing. Not doing the testing has provided a source of
  attention and income for Pye.
  My opinion is based on an close and personal one-on-one
  interview with the guy.
  Coming from a background in anthropology/archeology, I asked him
  specifics about the recovery of the artifact. The specifics are
  fairly hazy and undocumented. He feigned that the discovery was
  made by a 15 year-old girl and the report is anecdotal at best.
  My opinion was, that if the skull was found in a cave, and
  depending how old the mining cave was, it was a fossilized
  animal. A DNA test would be able to determine what genera the
  fossil belongs to. Simple and straight forward.

  KK

Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

  Gotta agree with KK here.
  This starchild thing has gone on longer than most wars.
  Why Pye lost $150k on this artifact is beyond me.
  If Coast to Coast AM really wanted to get to the bottom of this
  mystery they could have easily coughed up the dough to get the
  DNA extracted and analyzed and put a fork in this skull once and
  for all.
  It's another one of those never ending_constant spirals_of unresolved
  mysterious stuff_that never ends but is good for topic and filler.
  Same with the stories about giants. Oh there are supposed to be
  bones and such and this and that but for some strange reason no
  one can produce any remains nor DNA.
  Over the past few years Jaime Maussan has been investigating a
  creature caught in Mexico. Jaime allowed me to grill him on the
  remains and the procedures. He didn't back off one iota and even
  encouraged me to ask the hard questions. Little by little and with
  his own money he dug into the research and the mystery of the
  remains has been unfolding.
  Mysteries left to fester for decades when simple testing is available
  as well as standard scientific testing procedures are not engaged
  just raises red flags all around.

  Best,

  "The first DNA test was done in late 1999 by a forensic lab in
  Canada because none of the six labs in the world at that time
  capable of analyzing ancient DNA (like the Starchild's) would
  touch it with the proverbial ten foot pole. Why? Because they
  were terrified--surprise, surprise--of peer disapproval! So we
  gambled on the forensic lab being able to do a competent job for
  us, but they couldn't. Not until three years later, when there
  were 24 labs capable of recovering ancient DNA, could we talk
  one of them into testing the Starchild, and that lab proved
  beyond any doubt that the Starchild's mother was human and its
  father was not. What that lab could not prove with the primer
  technology available at that time was how far the Starchild's
  father, and therefore the Starchild itself, was from being
  human. That is what we can prove now with the new recovery
  technique from 454 Life Sciences, and that is what we will prove
  as soon as the required money is in hand to initiate it."
  --Comments made by Lloyd Ply:
  http://tinyurl.com/mgu52k

  Cathy asked:
  >Hold on a minute - what kind of DNA test are we talking about here?
  When Danny Penman contacted me for a comment I suggested he
  should have the skull examined by Professor Chris Stringer, of
  the Natural History Museum in London. Stringer is a renowned
  anthropologist and palaeontologist who would have the resources
  to carry out whatever genetic tests are required to make an
  informed comment on its likely origin. I understood Kathy's
  response to be much on the same lines.
  I made it quite clear to Danny that, in the absence of such
  results my opinion on the subject was of no consequence
  whatsoever. At which point he changed the subject and asked what
  I thought of Eric von Daniken's theories about visits from
  ancient astronauts. My response to this question appears to be
  what has been interpolated into the mish-mash published by "News
  Monster" that Dave Haith posted yesterday.
  From reading the resultant story it's clear that Danny didn't
  bother to contact Professor Stringer or, if he did, decided not
  to use his comments.

  also read on his Web site at LloydPye.com
  http://www.alienjigsaw.com/Articles/LloydPyesStarchildSkull.htm

  I don't understand why people who are commenting here don't
  do the following:
  (1) Read his book.
  (2) Read his updated article.
  (3) Do a Google search on "nuclear DNA" Neanderthal and educate
  yourself about the complexities of DNA testing of this type.
  Neanderthal DNA has been around for a lot longer than the star
  child skull and scientists just completed that analysis in 2006
  - 2007.
  I'm not an archeologist or a DNA Expert or anything else that
  people who are debunking this claim to be, but I'm willing to
  wait for the next step in the analysis of the skull to be done
  and published.

  K. Wilson

  >would be headline news across the world.
  Trouble is, this sort of reasoning is completely circular. It
  isn't headline news, therefore it has no credibility; it has no
  credibility, therefore it doesn't make the headlines; and so on,
  ad infinitum.
  In any case, you surely can't mean that the probability of an
  item making headline news is in any way proportional to its
  credibility ;-)

  >Scientific American. etc. and carbon 14 and DNA labs across the
  >world would fighting to subject it to expert analysis.
  If you want to see how the scientific press really does handle
  this sort of problem, check out the ongoing controversy over
  Homo Floriensis (aka the "hobbit").
  >The fact that even the Daily Mail spiked it in preference to
  >Michael Jackson's demise says all there is to say.
  About human nature, maybe.

  Cathy

                                Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics
  From: William Treurniet <wtreurniet.nul>
  Date: Tue, 22 Sep 2009 19:55:03 -0400
  Archived: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 14:11:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

  >From: Katharina Wilson <K_Wilson.nul>
  >To: <post.nul>,"Lloyd Pye" <lloydpye.nul>
  >Date: Tue, 22 Sep 2009 10:23:04 -0500
  >Subject: Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics
  >Here's Lloyd Pye's update article about the skull, which you can
  >also read on his Web site at LloydPye.com
  >http://www.alienjigsaw.com/Articles/LloydPyesStarchildSkull.htm
  >I don't understand why people who are commenting here don't do
  >the following:
  >(1) Read his book.
  >(2) Read his updated article.
  >(3) Do a Google search on "nuclear DNA" Neanderthal and educate
  >yourself about the complexities of DNA testing of this type.
  >Neanderthal DNA has been around for a lot longer than the star
  >child skull and scientists just completed that analysis in 2006
  >- 2007.
  >I'm not an archeologist or a DNA Expert or anything else that
  >people who are debunking this claim to be, but I'm willing to
  >wait for the next step in the analysis of the skull to be done
  >and published.
  The article at the above URL is my first exposure to the
  evidence. It strikes me that a pretty strong case has been made
  already, even without further DNA analysis, that there is
  something non-human about the skull. In particular, the fibers
  inside the bone, the unusual hardness of the bone, and the
  highly unusual but symmetric shape of the eye sockets do it for
  me.
  I recall from my basic biology education that phenotype is
  affected by environment. However, someone would need to explain
  what environmental conditions would cause strong fibers to be
  added to the bone structure, as well as the unusual placement of
  the openings for the optic nerve in both eyes. As Mr. Pye says,
  these must be due to genotype. Further DNA analysis would, of
  course, test this hypothesis, but the results are likely to be
  somewhat anti-climactic.
  Controversy seems unnecessary at this point. As both Katharina
  and Mr. Pye have said, we need but wait.

  William

  story - and use the same plea to solicit money from the public -
  has kept Pye in business for 10 years.

This Week At Inexplicata - 23-09-09




                                                          UFO Updates
                                      A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena

                                                This Week At Inexplicata - 23-09-09
  From: Scott Corrales <lornis1.nul>
  Date: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 13:35:20 -0400
  Archived: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 15:17:00 -0400
  Subject: This Week At Inexplicata - 23-09-09

  The Journal of Hispanic Ufology
  September 23, 2009
  This Week at INEXPLICATA:
  www.inexplicata.blogspot.com

  Argentina: A UFO Close Encounter in San Juan
  UFO "Studies"
  Two Individuals during a Close Encounter in San Juan
  By Sebasti=E1n Aranguren and Pachi la Fata
  The woman was sharing a moment of rest and conversation with a
  friend in a stand of pine trees in the province of San Juan. A
  strong wind had left in its wake a peaceful evening with clear
  skies. Both protagonists chatted beside a car when suddenly, a
  light having an estimated 5 meters in diameter lit up over their
  heads. When they looked up, the saw a floating object that
  appeared to "study" them for 10 seconds before taking off.
  Startled, they could not believe the experience they had just
  undergone, but they weren't alone: witnesses to this phenomenon
  could be counted in the dozens.

  Argentina: A "Firestarting" UFO in the Calamuchita Valley
  (Cordoba)?
  Readers of INEXPLICATA will remember the prevalence of accounts
  involving "fire-starting" UFOs in Argentina.
  Whether these blazes are deliberate, malicious acts or a result
  of materialization into our reality remains unknown.
  UFO photographer and videographer Martha Nunez managed to take
  an impressive photo of one such "firestarter" at noon on August
  29, 2009. Amid the dense gray smoke it is possible to make out a
  strange, spindle-shaped object among the hills, maintaing a
  horizontal position and apparently emitting light. An analysis
  was made by Jorge Luis Figueras.
  "This sighting", writes contributing editor Guillermo Gimenez,
  "is reminiscent of the other UFO photographed during the 5000
  hectare fire of January 24, 2008 in Villa Ventana (Province of
  Buenos Aires) and which appeared in the "Noticias Tornquist"
  local newspaper."
  Strange Panamanian Creature is a Sloth

  Strange Panamanian Creature is a Sloth
  By Paulo R. Poian
  The animal was already dead and decomposing, and the story was
  concocted by teenagers. The case was debunked last week by
  members of Brazil's Equipo UFO.



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m23-006.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:28]
This Week At Inexplicata - 23-09-09

  The story became widespread last Thursday, with a subject
  initially identified with Panama's location of Cerro Azul,
  involving the alleged death of a creature stoned to death by
  four adolescents near a lake. The unidentified creature was
  described as an alien, as if this classification offered an
  exlpanation. According to Panamanian papers, the youngsters were
  frightened by its appearance and fearful of being attacked,
  stoned it to death and then left it in a lake. The worst aspect
  of the story is that a so-called wildlife expert said that the
  case was under investigation and that the creature had very odd
  characteristics.

  I don't understand why these 'experts' (awful word) are
  (supposedly) so squeamish. Somebody presents a sample - they
  give an opinion. End of story. None of them is going to say
  'this is an alien hybrid skull' or 'this is an alien hybrid DNA
  sample' - they can't, because they don't have any alien hybrid
  skulls or DNA to act as a correlate. So they'll just give as
  much of an opinion as the sample and their expertise allow. Why
  should that be such a problem?
  Is it because they don't trust Pye, should their opinion be
  'this is an unknown', not to rush off and say 'this is an alien
  hybrid and Professor X or Dr. Y says so!'?
  Maybe Pye needs to offer to indemnify these experts against any
  loss of professional standing arising from misrepresentation of
  their findings on his part - or maybe he has and I am unaware of
  it.

  Gerald O'Connell

Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 23

                                Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics
  From: David Clarke <daveclarke292.nul>
  Date: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 20:09:27 +0100
  Archived: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 16:23:00 -0400
  Subject: Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

  >From: Dave Haith <visions1.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates <post.nul>
  >Date: Tue, 22 Sep 2009 23:53:38 +0100
  >Subject: Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics
  >So let's get this clear David. If Pye produces experts who do
  >DNA testing and make pronouncements on the skull, these 'don't
  >stand up' as you put it.
  Quite clearly, otherwise there would be no ambiguity about the
  origins of the skull and we wouldn't be having this discussion.
  Pye is quoted in the Danny Dyer article as saying that "genetic
  tests" (type not specified) had revealed "the creature's father
  was not human although his mother certainly was."
  Yet a basic web search turns up this statement: "...scientific
  testing has shown the skull is that of a male child who died
  approximately 900 years ago. DNA testing has shown that both his
  parents were human." (Wikipedia)
  Which suggests to me there is some disagreement between
  "experts". Usually the best way to resolve such ambiguity is to
  get an independent set of results. Hence my suggestion that
  Professor Stringer might help provide that.
  >But if he chooses the expert you suggest to do the tests then
  >you might be willing to listen to this 'informed comment'?
  I'm trying to be helpful by suggesting someone I think should be
  able to give a definitive opinion.
  Professor Stringer's qualifications speak for themselves, see:
  http://www.nhm.ac.uk/research-curation/staff-directory/palaeontology/c-stringer/index.html
  I don't understand why what I think or don't think about the
  skull is so important. Who cares what I think, other than you
  and Danny Dyer?
  Speaking personally, I've read as much as I need to read and I
  remain unconvinced the skull is an "alien human hybrid", as
  claimed by Pye.
  Judging by the reaction of several others on this list, they
  remain equally unconvinced.

  >several times - hasn't already shown it to the good Professor
  >Stringer, he'll be more than happy to - especially if with you
  >as a contact, he could get a free DNA check.
  I'd be happy to do what I can to put Pye in touch with Professor
  Stringer, if paying for a test is a problem. If nothing else,
  you've piqued my journalistic curiosity to follow this up for
  you.

  >Stringer might not leap at the chance to make headlines over

  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>

  Subject: UFO? Mystery Airplane Buzzes The Banks

  http://tinyurl.com/mrpx4h

  This story is just too amazing to be believed.
  It has nothing to do with the ETH but it's about some
  mysterious, large aircraft that has made several near touch and
  go maneuvers at the Dare County Regional Airport.
  What's equally alarming is that no one is taking responsibility
  for this craft which the story reports has been leaking some
  sort of milky white substance on ground vehicles and aircraft
  below it's flight path.
  Even more alarming is that the story states the military doesn't
  always inform the airport when they do maneuvers like this.
  The military hasn't admitted this craft is one of their own so
  who the heck is doing this? What type of airport safety
  protocols do we not know about?
  What's in this milky residue this craft is leaving behind?

                                                  UFO Documentary 'It Has Begun'
  From: Greg Boone <Evolbaby.nul>
  Date: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 18:38:55 EDT
  Archived: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 04:05:41 -0400
  Subject: UFO Documentary 'It Has Begun'

  Google Video - 90 mins:
  http://tinyurl.com/yalbrqx

  For some strange reason either I missed this video or did and am
  getting senile.
  It has everything any true student of ufology would want. It's
  narrated by the late great Rod Serling, it features guests such
  as Burgess Meredith and Jose Ferrer and the entire documentary
  is chock full of the people and professionals who were at launch
  zero of the UFO/flying saucer era.
  It's got lengthy on camera interviews with Dr. J. Allen Hynek,
  Col. Friend, Jacques Vallee, officer Lonnie Zamora and on and on
  as I don't want to ruin all the surprises!
  If you haven't seen it and don't have it in your collection it's
  an absolute must have and in it's own right presents an
  abundance of evidence in regard to theories and conclusions of
  UFO data.
  The surprise was that even the panel consisting of Dr. Hynek did
  present their theories that the ETH isn't and shouldn't be the
  only hypothesis presented. Also that they admit we just may not
  have the smarts and tools to solve this issue at present.
  Needless to say it covers all the bases and more. Quite timely
  for today's audiences and people venturing into the UFO research
  field.

  Best,
  Greg
  ufomafia.com/blog

                                          John E. Mack Video Interview For INREES
  From: Sergey Shpakovsky <sergejsh.nul>
  Date: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 20:25:30 -0400
  Archived: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 05:13:00 -0400
  Subject: John E. Mack Video Interview For INREES

  * * *
  INREES - Institute For Research on Extraordinary Experiences,
  France. The Institute was founded by journalist and writer
  Stephane Allix and Doctor Bernard Castells.

  John Edward Mack, M.D. video interview
  (Following descriptions are translated from French)
  Part 1 (3:05 min) - John Mack talks about his work with the
  psychiatrist Stanislav Grof, which led him to discover
  trans-personal psychology:
  http://tinyurl.com/lxx3zl
  Part 2 (2:49 min) - John Mack about his meeting with Budd
  Hopkins and his first questions about extraterrestrial
  encounters:
  http://tinyurl.com/npmh53
  Part 3 (2:00 min) - John Mack about first encounter with
  experiencers and the reality of experience:
  http://tinyurl.com/lze9je
  Part 4 (2:32 min) - John Mack explains how experiencers do not
  suffer from schizophrenia or other personality disorders:
  http://tinyurl.com/lhx76c
  Part 5 (1:25 min) - John Mack discusses state of consciousness
  of the abductes:
  http://tinyurl.com/m4n5jm
  Part 6 (1:44 min) - John Mack explains why witnesses are not
  hallucinating. All events that occur show incredible
  consistency:
  http://tinyurl.com/lor8lq
  Part 7 (0:28 min) - John Mack recalls the definition of fantasy
  and the inconsistency of this hypothesis against all the
  evidence:
  http://tinyurl.com/kl9clc
  Part 8 (0:25 min) - John Mack speaks of a very important point
  in his diagnosis: the sincerity and intensity of evidence.
  People who describe these events are perfectly sane and know
  what they say seems crazy:
  http://tinyurl.com/nww44w
  Part 9 (1:59 min) - John Mack notes the failure of our world.
  These events which are not supposed to happen in our culture


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m24-003.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:31]
John E. Mack Video Interview For INREES

  shows a real problem - the limits of our ontological structure:
  http://tinyurl.com/mj6dq9
  Part 10 (3:03 min) - John Mack sais that the definition of
  reality is constructed in relation to our culture. This is true
  with us but is completely different from what is true in other
  cultures:
  http://tinyurl.com/nzohqj
  Part 11 (3:33 min) - John Mack reveals his skepticism about
  prevailing worldview. The programming of the brain based on a
  materialistic scientific knowledge is in contradiction with some
  experiences of many people:
  http://tinyurl.com/ngekqs
  Part 12 (3:16 min) - John Mack explains his working method, the
  first clinical evaluation of patients at meetings and interviews
  conducted in the state of relaxation:
  http://tinyurl.com/nsocsc
  Part 13 (1:36 min) - John Mack says that after hundreds of cases
  encountered, he saw himself designed the structure of a common
  history, similar in many countries:
  http://tinyurl.com/ngolqh
  Part 14 (0:40 min) - John Mack reveals the difficulty in
  fighting against prejudice:
  http://tinyurl.com/klo6k4

Three Spacecraft Find Water On The Moon




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 24

                                          Three Spacecraft Find Water On The Moon
  From: Stanton T Friedman <fsphys.nul>
  Date: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 09:12:23 -0300
  Archived: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 11:07:26 -0400
  Subject: Three Spacecraft Find Water On The Moon

  Another victory for truth. How many have claimed there is no
  water on the moon?
  Stan Friedman
  -----
  Source: Custom Briefings.com
  http://tinyurl.com/y9pz9ek
  September 24, 2009

  Three Spacecraft Find Water On The Moon.
  Three spacecraft have indications of widespread water on the
  moon. Media coverage focused on the surprise of scientists
  regarding this result and how it has potential implications for
  future human exploration. ABC World News (9/23, story 10, 1:45,
  Gibson) reported, "It appears there is much more water on the
  surface of the moon than anyone ever knew." ABC (Ned Potter)
  added, "Today's discovery is something that NASA has been hoping
  for, for years." Carl Pilcher, NASA: "Water that might be used
  someday by...human lunar explorers, who return to the moon to do
  science on the moon and perhaps it is a steppingstone out into
  the rest of the solar system." Potter: "Several satellites
  orbiting the moon have found tell tale readings of ice in the
  lunar soil, billions of gallons of it. There had been hints of
  this in the 1990s, but now, three papers in the journal
  'Science' say there is good proof. ... In just a few weeks,
  there may be even better proof. NASA plans to crash a rocket
  called LCROSS into the moon, and a satellite will measure
  precisely what it kicks up."
  The AP (9/24, Borenstein) reports the water "was spotted by
  spacecraft that either circled the moon or flew by. All three
  ships used the same type of instrument that looked at the
  absorption of a specific wavelength of light that is the
  chemical signature of only two molecules: water and hydroxyl,"
  but scientists "doubted the unexpected measurement until it was
  confirmed independently and repeatedly." Measurements first seen
  aboard India's Chandrayaan-1 sent scientists looking at the data
  from NASA's Deep Impact and Cassini spacecraft for confirmation
  because they thought the measurements were wrong. "The chance
  that three different instruments malfunctioned in the same way
  on three different spaceships is almost zilch, so this confirms
  that it's water and hydroxyl," Brown University's Carle "Pieters
  said." However, "if you want a drink of water, it would take a
  baseball diamond's worth of dirt, said...Pieters." The AP notes
  that other scientists not involved with the work also called the
  findings "unequivocal."
  Lawrence Taylor of the University of Tennessee, Knoxville,
  speaking in the New York Times (9/24, A29, Chang), said, "It's
  so startling because it's so pervasive." Widespread water "could
  make future settlement of the Moon easier, especially if
  significant water could be extracted just by heating the soil."
  Samples from the Apollo mission "did show signs of water, but


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m24-005.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:32]
Three Spacecraft Find Water On The Moon

  most scientists working with the samples, including Dr. Taylor,
  dismissed the readings," but now he is "convinced he was wrong."
  The data "suggests the water is being created when protons from
  the solar wind slam into the lunar surface. The collisions may
  free oxygen atoms in the minerals and allow them to recombine
  with protons and electrons to form water."
  The Los Angeles Times (9/24, Johnson) notes that Roger Clark of
  the US Geological Survey, author of one of the papers, said "the
  discovery 'will forever change how we look at the moon.'"
  According to the article, "At the very least, the discovery
  lends weight to a new view of a friendlier solar system, where
  water...suddenly seems to be everywhere." However, it is
  "unclear how this discovery will affect the debate in Washington
  over NASA's future, but the presence of water on the moon would
  presumably make colonization much easier."
  Space.com (9/24, Thompson) reports, "The various study
  researchers also suggest that the daily dehydration and
  rehydration of the trace water across the surface could lead to
  the migration of hydroxyl and hydrogen towards the poles where
  it can accumulate in the cold traps of the permanently shadowed
  regions."
  The Christian Science Monitor (9/23, Shaer) "Horizons" blog,
  Reuters (9/24), Bloomberg News (9/24, Johnson), Discovery News
  (9/23, Klotz), and Science News (9/23, Cowen) also cover the
  story.

  >The defense lawyer for McKinnon is going to blame the various

  >Hey, it used to work to blame the victim of rape. It was her

  >My, my, but I think McKinnon needs to get better representation
  >than the attorney he now has.
  As someone who has been in the computer industry for the past 25
  years, my take on the McKinnon case is that the dolts in charge
  of computer security for the systems Gary hacked into are at
  least 80% to blame.
  The accounts I've read on how he got into those systems indicate
  that the security of these systems ranged from non-existent to
  elementary. He has related that he was not the only one
  rummaging through those computers, but he was the only one
  stupid enough to leave behind traces that eventually identified
  him to the U.S. authorities.
  For example: http://tinyurl.com/ybdmq6h
  So I agree in part with his attorney - the IT personnel who were
  supposed to be actually securing those computers should also be
  on trial. Attacking the criminally bad security of those
  computers is an excellent defence strategy.
  This does not excuse McKinnon's actions, but it is difficult to
  brand him "The World's Most Dangerous Hacker" when the systems
  he broke into were so poorly secured, and he was un-skilled
  enough in the art of hacking to leave behind identifiable
  traces.

  Bruce Hutchinson

                                Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics
  From: Gerald O'Connell <goc.nul>
  Date: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 09:56:09 +0100
  Archived: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 13:14:21 -0400
  Subject: Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

  >From: David Clarke <daveclarke292.nul>
  >To: UFO UpDates - Toronto <post.nul>
  >Date: Wed, 23 Sep 2009 20:09:27 +0100
  >Subject: Re: Starchild And What Annoys Me About The Sceptics

  >Pye is quoted in the Danny Dyer article as saying that "genetic
  >tests" (type not specified) had revealed "the creature's father
  >was not human although his mother certainly was."
  >Yet a basic web search turns up this statement: "...scientific
  >testing has shown the skull is that of a male child who died
  >approximately 900 years ago. DNA testing has shown that both his
  >parents were human." (Wikipedia)
  This is a non-trivial discrepancy.
  Some superficial digging around reveals:
  The Wikipedia piece is at:
  http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starchild_skull
  It references:
  http://www.theness.com/the-starchild-project/
  for the assertion that both parents were human.
  There is currently no acknowledgment of the work by Dr. David
  Sweet that supposedly supports this conclusion on the Starchild
  website, but Google has a cached result from the Starchild
  website at:
  http://tinyurl.com/y8ukdwo
  which refers to the DNA work done by Dr. David Sweet.
  So it appears that Pye has recently withdrawn the reference in
  his acknowledgements. The reasons for this are a matter for
  conjecture.
  It would be informative if somebody on the list who is genuinely
  expert in the field of genetics and DNA testing could examine
  the various claims and counterclaims flying around on this one,
  and then enlighten us as to their status. Right now I have that
  awful yet familiar feeling that all of the protagonists, be they
  sceptic or supporter, are being selective in their reference to,
  and interpretation of, the evidence thus far assembled.
  --
  Gerald O'Connell
  http://www.onlyport.com

  >Subject: Re: News Links - 22-09-09
  >>News Links - 22-09-09
  >>Expert Challenges UFO Hacker's $700k Bill
  >>ComputerWeekly.com
  >>http://tinyurl.com/kp9tdv
  >The defense lawyer for McKinnon is going to blame the various
  >U.S. agencies for not having the right kind of firewall to
  >prevent McKinnon's intrusion into their databases.
  >Hey, it used to work to blame the victim of rape. It was her
  >fault she got raped.
  >I guess McKinnon's defense attorney thinks that argument should
  >work in court here in the U.S. when the trial begins. It was the
  >U.S. agencies fault McKinnon was able to get into their systems.
  >My, my, but I think McKinnon needs to get better representation
  >than the attorney he now has.
  Suppose you left the front door open while on vacation, and
  someone enters and steals information from private files. You
  would argue that your privacy was violated and you had been
  wronged. Then you install a home security system. Should the
  burglar, when apprehended, be liable for the cost of that
  security system? If you argue that he should be held liable,
  then should he also pay for all the systems installed in houses
  that have never been robbed?
  It seems to me that the US government is arguing the latter in
  McKinnon's case. The $700K in damages must be the cost of
  belatedly installing a security system across the whole affected
  organization. You might be fine with that claim, but others
  might see it as inappropriate punishment.

  wct

  not be victim of a similar tragedy in the future.

  dim-witted anyway, and made a good example.

Hastings On Coast to Coast - 30-09-09




                                                          UFO Updates
                                    A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                                'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 25

                                              Hastings On Coast to Coast - 30-09-09
  From: Robert Hastings <hastings444.nul>
  Date: Thu, 24 Sep 2009 19:11:48 -0600
  Archived: Fri, 25 Sep 2009 07:55:53 -0400
  Subject: Hastings On Coast to Coast - 30-09-09


  FOR RELEASE TO THE MEDIA

  Noted UFO Researcher Robert Hastings To Appear On Coast To Coast
  Am To Discuss Important Revelations About The Roswell Incident

  UFO-Nukes Connection researcher Robert Hastings will make his
  third appearance on Coast to Coast AM on Wednesday, September
  30, 2009. (The program begins at 10 p.m. Pacific Daylight Time
  but Hastings' one-hour segment begins at 1 a.m. Thursday morning
  PDT.) Visit:
  http://www.coasttocoastam.com/
  for details.
  The topic for discussion will be former U.S. Atomic Energy
  Commission Supervisor Chet Lytle's revelations about the Roswell
  Incident. Lytle, who did engineering work for the Manhattan
  Project, which created the first atomic bomb during World War
  II, states that the former Roswell Army Airfield commander,
  then-Colonel William Blanchard, personally told him that the
  crashed object secretly recovered near the base, in July 1947,
  was an alien spacecraft.
  A detailed discussion of Lytle's admission appears in Hastings'
  critically-acclaimed book UFO and Nukes: Extraordinary
  Encounters at Nuclear Weapons Sites. A brief summary may also be
  found at:
  http://tinyurl.com/me3nje
  The dramatic revelation occurred during a taped interview with
  Hastings, concerning Lytle's knowledge of nuclear weapons-
  related UFO activity. Unexpectedly, the retired engineer began
  discussing Roswell. In February 1953, Lytle - by then a weapons
  stockpiling supervisor with the Atomic Energy Commission (AEC) -
  was visiting Eielson Air Force Base, Alaska, together with
  newly-promoted USAF Brigadier General Blanchard, whose
  assignment was the training of aircrews for the B-36 strategic
  bombers based there.
  According to Lytle, who held Top Secret clearances related to
  nuclear weapons work, during a flight from Alaska to Illinois,
  his "very good friend" Blanchard confessed to him that an
  extraterrestrial spacecraft had been recovered in 1947, together
  with four dead aliens. Lytle, whose AEC office at Wright-
  Patterson AFB, in the early 1950s, put him in the right place at
  the right time to learn more, said that he had also been privy
  to "leaks about autopsies on the bodies from people who had been
  in and seen them." Lytle further stated that the crashed craft
  had been stored at Hanger 5 on the base, adding, "I had the
  highest clearances involving atomic weapons stockpiling. I was
  very heavily cleared to do what I did, but I was never allowed
  into that area."
  Hastings says, "Three years after my 1998 interview with Lytle,


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m25-001.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:34]
Hastings On Coast to Coast - 30-09-09

  he refused to answer a few follow-up questions, nervously
  telling me that after our first conversation he had been 'paid a
  visit' by someone who wanted to know why he was talking to me
  and Roswell researcher Don Schmitt."

  Archived: Sat, 26 Sep 2009 10:21:55 -0400
  Subject: Re: Three Spacecraft Find Water On The Moon

  >From: Stanton T Friedman <fsphys.nul>

  Hi Everyone,
  In addition to the Earth, there are many other bodies in our
  Solar System that are known or suspected of having large
  quantities of water ice on or just below their surfaces. These
  bodies include other "moons" such as Jupiter's Europa (a world
  compariable in size to our Moon) and Saturn's Enceladus (only
  500 kilometers in diameter) that also have very thin or no
  atmospheres.
  The news that ice water ice was discovered on the Moon did not
  come as a surprise to York's space scientists who confirmed with
  their experiments on the Phoenix Mars lander that there is lots
  of water ice on and just below the surface of Mars and even
  observed water ice clouds and falling snow.
  In fact, plans are already underway for a joint U.S./Canadian
  spacecraft/rover that will land in or near a crater located at
  the Moon's South Pole that is in perpetual shadow which is
  filled with water ice. Involved in this future joint lunar
  mission is the head of the U.S. Department of Defense funded
  Clementine mission (1994) to the Moon.
  Interestingly, "Clementine" was the name of the large claw at
  the end of a three mile long arm that came out from underneath
  the ship Glomar Explorer which was used to secretly retrieve a
  sunken Soviet submarine near Hawaii and bring it up from its
  dark watery grave into the ships hidden "Moon Bay" hold. If we
  could do this back in the late 1960s, we can use a similar claw
  to retrieve water ice from inside this dark lunar crater and
  convince those few scientists who want to see before they can
  believe that there is indeed water on the Moon.
  If global warming on Earth is real, I would not be surprised if
  sometime in the near future Winter Olympics events such as ice
  hockey and downhill skiing are held on the slopes and surface of
  this water ice filled lunar crater which has the potential of
  sustaining a large human settlement - another Whistler, B.C.?
  With water settlers can grow food and wash their lunar cars and
  through the electrolysis of water (solar cell powered energy is
  available 24 hours a day, 7 days a week from the rim of this
  lunar crater) they can also produce Oxygen gas to breath and
  Hydrogen gas to heat their homes or be used as fuel for their
  spacecraft, etc.

                                            Brazil Declassifies More UFO Documents
  From: A. J. Gevaerd - Revista UFO <aj.nul>
  Date: Sat, 26 Sep 2009 11:11:12 -0300
  Archived: Sat, 26 Sep 2009 13:23:02 -0400
  Subject: Brazil Declassifies More UFO Documents

  Dear Colleagues:
  This is to inform that Brazilian Government has just
  declassified a new set of previously secret UFO documents,
  covering the 80s. We already had disclosures covering the 50s,
  60s and 70s. So far over 4,000 pages have been disclosed.
  The recent disclosure is very powerful because it contains
  dozens of reports of UFOs on May 19, 1986, considered the
  'Official UFO Night in Brazil', when 21 spherical objects,
  estimated 100 meters in diameter - according to military sources
  - literally jammed air traffic over the major Braziian Airports,
  when several Mirage and F5 jets were scrambled to pursue them.
  As a result, the Air Force minister went public about it the
  other morning on the national TV network. The pilots and their
  commanders also spoke freely about the pursuits, which occured
  over several hours.
  We've had several other disclosures since 2007, all as a direct
  result of the campaign UFOs: Freedom Of Information Now,
  promoted since 2004 by the Brazilian Committee of UFO
  Researchers, of which I am the coordinator. The latest
  disclosure comprises over 2,200 new pages of formerly secret UFO
  documents that were given to the Committee and also sent to the
  National Archives.
  In late July, hundreds of papers from the Brazilian Air Force's
  official System for the Investigation of Unidentified Aerial
  Phenomenon (SIOANI) have demonstrated how the nation's military
  authorities dealt with the UFO Phenomena in the 60s and 70s.
  Considering that SIOANI wasn't a top secret agency and that even
  civilian researchers took part in its procedures, the structure
  and functionality of which proves that Brazil is the first
  country in the world to officially and publicly admit the
  reality of the UFO Phenomena, and the first to investigate it
  openly.
  The Brazilian UFO Magazine website - www.ufo.com.br - already
  has thousands of pages for download, such as over 1,200 pages of
  documents, 200 photos of SIOANI's activities and Operation
  Saucer, carried out in the Amazonian region in 1977. Along with
  documents received by the Committee from other military sources.
  Included in the recently revealed documents are the detailed
  research proceedings carried out in dozens of UFO-related
  incidents which had not been made public previously. Some of the
  colored sketches of UFO incidents investigated by SIOANI are
  here:
  www.ufo.com.br/documentos/sioani/Novos_documentos
  Other documents can be found here:
  www.ufo.com.br/documentos/sioani/
  www.ufo.com.br/documentos/prato/
  www.ufo.com.br/documentos/extra/
  www.ufo.com.br/documentos/geral/



http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m26-005.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:39]

  Dear UFO UpDate Subscribers,
  After a long wait I have finally succeeded in getting my ISP to
  run DSL High Speed into my area. I live out in the country in an
  area - a pocket of recreational homes - that is separated from the
  mainstream.
  They created a footprint for me after I bugged them for several
  months and they satisfied themselves that the new line's cost
  will be justified by the number of new customers it will serve.
  I'm thrilled with the results.
  I'd like to thank those who took pity on a dial-up-er and
  supplied me with what would have been large file downloads on
  CDs over the last few years, which for me these would be
  impossible to download.
  Perhaps now I will be able to pay these kindnesses forward.
  Again, thanks.

  Don Ledger

Ray Stanford's Comments To UFO Iconoclast




                                                          UFO Updates
                                   A mailing list for the study of UFO-related phenomena
                                               'Its All Here In Black & White'
    Location: UFOUpDatesList.Com > 2009 > Sep > Sep 27

                                        Ray Stanford's Comments To UFO Iconoclast
  From: Ray Stanford <opus22.nul>
  Date: Sun, 27 Sep 2009 01:48:45 -0400
  Archived: Sun, 27 Sep 2009 10:42:00 -0400
  Subject: Ray Stanford's Comments To UFO Iconoclast

  I have sent the following to The UFO Iconoclast, in response to
  Anthony Bragalia's ludicrous claim that the Socorro CE III case
  was engineered by anonymous college students. I doubt it will
  get posted, or at least it hasn't as yet.:
  -----
  The claims made in The Socorro UFO Hoax Exposed! - Famous 1964
  Sighting Was A College Prank -) by Anthony Bragalia would be
  unacceptable as evidence in any court of law. His article
  contains only hear-say, and no evidence of what is alleged to
  have happened has been brought forth.
  Any reader with the ability to hear can have the chance to learn
  the facts on Friday, October 2, at 1:30 to 3:30 PM, eastern USA
  time. That's because I have kindly been invited to address this
  matter on The Paracast at that time.
  Bragalia's claim that the Socorro CE III case has been exposed
  should never have been published as being fact.
  Please note: I wrote the book on the Socorro case, and that's a
  literal statement -- all 211 pages of it.
  On Wednesday, April 29, 1964, I was the only investigator
  invited by Dr. J. Allen Hynek to be with him, Lonnie Zamora, and
  New Mexico State Police Sergeant Samuel Chavez (No one else was
  allowed there.) while we went over the entire experience.
  Zamora didn't like to give many details to 'outsiders', but this
  time was different since he felt it his patriotic duty because
  Hynek was the official investigator for the U.S. Air Force.
  Lonnie even sat in the police car parked exactly where it had
  been during the close-range part of the event, and went through
  the entire bodily action of quickly exiting the police car,
  stepping fast with his prescription glasses and sunshades on,
  directly toward the object. Then, as he did again for us, at
  only about thirty-five feet from where the 18-to-20 foot
  ellipsoidal object had rested, Zamora threw himself into a
  protective prone position on the slope, because a sudden and
  terribly loud roar began as a blue column of brilliant light
  emitted from the object. It was a diverging cone of light, being
  narrow at the top, but wider toward the ground.
  Even at ~ 35 feet away, Zamora could feel heat radiating from
  the luminous 'exhaust'. Something has to be extremely hot to be
  felt at that distance, especially with a stiff breeze blowing it
  in another direction.
  Subsequently, Allen Hynek commended my 1976, 211-page book about
  the case in a letter to me, intended for use in promoting it. An
  electronic copy of Hynek's letter is available from me, at
  anyone's individual request. He also donated a copy of my
  Socorro book to the technical library of NASA's Lyndon B.
  Johnson Space Center. (I have proof of that.)
  My position regarding the Socorro object: It was an 18-to-20
  foot ellipsoid of high eccentricity (i.e., long, rather narrow).


http://www.ufoupdateslist.com/2009/sep/m27-004.shtml[11/12/2011 19:27:41]
Ray Stanford's Comments To UFO Iconoclast

  It displayed two distinct propulsion modes. One was used only in
  landing and take-off and was very noisy. The other and
  apparently primary propulsion mode was, by contrast, very quiet,
  but it imparted very high speed (both horizontally and in almost
  vertical flight) to the object in very few seconds of time.
  The source of the object is unknown. Although it was
  technologically sophisticated, that does not certify an extra-
  terrestrial origin, although associated evidences might, and not
  unreasonably, be taken as evidence of such.
  Publicly unknown but extremely well-documented evidence relevant
  to the question of exotic propulsion of the Socorro object was
  recovered from the Socorro landing site and is in my possession.
  Its nature and possible significance will be discussed on The
  Paracast, October 2.

  claims about the Socorro case. It will be published on any
  willing website when ready, because I don't personally have time
  to maintain one. Hopefully even Bragalia may be willing to carry
  it, once he learns the facts.
  Ray Stanford
